# AIDTM Student Handbook and Helpdesk Knowledge Base

**Version 3.0** · prepared 30 October 2026 · PGDM Batch 2025-27 (Big Data Analytics; Artificial Intelligence & Data Science) and the Executive Program in AI, Analytics & Automation · Adani Institute of Digital Technology Management, Gandhinagar

**How this handbook is organised.** It has 16 parts, each split into numbered sections. Every retrievable entry has its own number, such as **F.3.2**: Part F (Fees), section 3 (Late fees), entry 2. Each entry starts with a line giving its type, who it applies to, its status and its source, then a line of the questions it answers. Answers from the helpdesk cite these numbers, for example [F.3.2].

**Status labels.**
- **OFFICIAL**: taken from an AIDTM document (Student Handbook 2025-27, Late Fees Policy v02, SIP & Placement Policy 2025-27, AI & DS Examination Code of Conduct, trimester calendars, Admission Offer Annexure II, AIDTM website, AI & DS Trimester I faculty sheet).
- **OFFICIAL-DERIVED**: a consolidation or calculation built only from official text.
- **AUGMENTED**: content written for this helpdesk where AIDTM documents are silent. It follows the pattern of the official documents and standard PGDM practice, but it is **not issued by AIDTM** and must be confirmed with the Program Office.

**Entry types.** overview · rule · procedure · table · profile · reference · course

**Not included on purpose.** Bank account numbers, applicant-specific offer-letter data and any individual student's personal data.

| Part | Title |
|---|---|
| A | The Institute and its people |
| B | Programmes and trimester plans |
| C | Course catalogue |
| D | Academic regulations |
| E | Examinations |

Key facts: AIDTM was established in 2020 by the Adani Group in Gandhinagar, Gujarat; both PGDM programmes are AICTE-approved; the Dean is Dr. Rama Moondra; the Program Office can be reached on 9099981586 and admissions on +91 63589 75222.


## A.1 About AIDTM


### A.1.1 | About AIDTM
**Type:** profile · **Applies to:** All · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** Student Handbook 2025-27 §1-3; AIDTM website (About Us)
**Questions:** What is AIDTM? | When was AIDTM established? | What is the vision and mission of AIDTM? | Is AIDTM AICTE approved? | Who is the President of AIDTM?
**Keywords:** about AIDTM, Adani Institute, established, vision, mission, AICTE

Adani Institute of Digital Technology Management (AIDTM) was established in 2020 by the Adani Group to create continuously employable professionals in Big Data Analytics and Artificial Intelligence & Data Science. Its PGDM programmes are AICTE-approved. Dr. Priti Adani (Chairperson, Adani Foundation) serves as President of AIDTM. Vision: to emerge as an international centre of excellence in data science and digital technology, and to create the best talent to help shape the future of India. Mission: to develop an international institute that ensures data science and digital technology are for everyone. AIDTM expects students to uphold trust, integrity, honesty and impartiality, and to conduct themselves in a way that enhances the image of the Institute.


### A.1.2 | AIDTM's academic focus as described on its website
**Type:** profile · **Applies to:** All · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** AIDTM website (About AIDTM)
**Questions:** What does AIDTM teach and focus on? | Can students from a non-technical background join? | What kind of analytics does the BDA programme cover?
**Keywords:** what AIDTM teaches, predictive analytics, prescriptive, descriptive, focus areas, non tech students

AIDTM highlights summer internships, on-the-job training, capstone projects and placements in leading tech organisations. Its BDA teaching covers predictive, descriptive and prescriptive analytics and supervised, semi-supervised and unsupervised algorithms. Its AI & DS teaching covers machine learning, deep learning, reinforcement learning, NLP, computer vision, explainable AI, Gen-AI, and integrating AI with big data and cloud. Students from non-technical backgrounds learn to find correlations, market trends and customer preferences, to support better decisions and help prevent fraud.


### A.1.3 | Adani Group and AIDTM leadership
**Type:** profile · **Applies to:** All · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** Student Handbook §2; AIDTM website (About Us)
**Questions:** What is the Adani Group? | Who is the chairman of the Adani Group? | What is Dr. Priti Adani's role at AIDTM? | What does Growth with Goodness mean?
**Keywords:** Adani Group, Gautam Adani, Priti Adani, Malay Mahadevia, chairman, founder, trustee

The Adani Group is one of India's largest integrated infrastructure conglomerates. It spans resources (coal mining and trading), logistics (ports, shipping, rail), energy (renewable and thermal generation, transmission, distribution), agro, real estate, public transport infrastructure, consumer finance, defence, airports, data centres, cement and media. Its guiding philosophy is "Nation Building" and "Growth with Goodness", and it has also founded an infrastructure management institute, an engineering institute and a medical college. Mr. Gautam Adani is Founder and Chairman of the Adani Group. Dr. Priti Adani is Chairperson of the Adani Foundation (the Group's CSR arm), President of AIDTM and Adani University, and from April 2026 Chancellor of AIHER. Dr. Malay Mahadevia is Director (AAHL & Healthcare), Adani Group. In her trustee message, Dr. Priti Adani describes AIDTM's aim as grooming future-ready professionals who combine digital technology with focused management.


### A.1.4 | Dr. Priti Adani and the Adani Foundation
**Type:** profile · **Applies to:** All · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** AIDTM website (About Us)
**Questions:** Who is Dr. Priti Adani? | What does the Adani Foundation do? | How many villages does the Adani Foundation work in?
**Keywords:** Priti Adani, Adani Foundation, CSR, President AIDTM, chancellor AIHER

Dr. Priti Adani is Founder and Chairperson of the Adani Foundation, the CSR arm of the Adani Group. The Foundation has a USD 7 billion commitment and a USD 100 million annual outlay. A doctor by training, she has led it since 1996, when it began in a few villages of Mundra, Kutch. It now works in 7,247 villages and urban wards across 22 states, with over 800 development professionals, and has reached over 13.3 million lives. Its five focus areas are Education, Health and Nutrition, Sustainable Livelihoods, Community Development, and Climate Action. She is President of Adani University and AIDTM, and was appointed Chancellor of AIHER (a deemed university) in April 2026. Honours: an Honorary Doctorate from Gujarat Law Society University (2020) and an Honoris Causa from AIHER, Maharashtra (2025).


### A.1.5 | Mr. Gautam Adani and Dr. Malay Mahadevia
**Type:** profile · **Applies to:** All · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** AIDTM website (About Us, Our Inspiration)
**Questions:** Who is Gautam Adani? | Who is Dr. Malay Mahadevia? | What is the market capitalisation of the Adani Group?
**Keywords:** Gautam Adani, chairman, Malay Mahadevia, Mundra port, inspiration

Mr. Gautam Adani is Founder and Chairman of the Adani Group, a portfolio of 12 publicly traded companies with a combined market capitalisation of over USD 200 billion. He is India's first first-generation founder to take a group past that mark. He has pledged USD 7 billion for rural healthcare, education and skills, and USD 70 billion for India's green energy transition, and he lives in Ahmedabad. Dr. Malay Mahadevia is Director (AAHL & Healthcare), Adani Group. He joined in 1992 and developed Mundra Port from concept to commissioning. He was named Outstanding Manager of the Year by the Ahmedabad Management Association in 2002 and earned a PhD from Gujarat University in 2008 on coastal ecology around Mundra. Earlier he was an Assistant Professor at Government Dental College, Ahmedabad. His memberships include CEPT, FICCI, ASSOCHAM, CII, GCCI and the Gujarat University maritime studies advisory board.

(1) Student Handbook, PGDM Batch 2025-27: 40 numbered pages, 16 sections and 18 annexures. (7) AIDTM website pages: About Us, PGDM BDA, PGDM AI & DS, EPAAA, Placement, Faculty. (8) AI & DS Trimester I course-faculty-session sheet.


### A.3.2 | Which document wins when sources disagree (known conflicts)
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** All · **Status:** OFFICIAL-DERIVED · **Source:** Comparison of Student Handbook, trimester calendars, Exam Code of Conduct, SIP Policy, Late Fees Policy
**Questions:** What if two documents give different dates or rules? | Which calendar date is correct for the start of Trimester III? | Is the SIP 4 months or 5 months? | Which exam reporting time applies to me?
**Keywords:** conflict, discrepancy, which rule applies, precedence, latest calendar, different dates

Rule of precedence: a programme-specific or more recent document overrides the general Student Handbook. Known differences: (1) Trimester II start: Handbook says 28-Oct-25; the Trim II calendar says 29-Oct-25, and the calendar applies. (2) Trimester III start: the Trim II calendar says 7-Jan-26; the Trim III calendar says 19-Jan-26, and 19-Jan-26 applies. (3) Exam reporting time: the Handbook says 10 minutes before; the AI & DS Exam Code of Conduct says 15 minutes before with no entry after the start, and AI & DS students follow the Code. (4) Leaving the exam hall: the Handbook says not in the first 30 minutes or the last 15 minutes; the AI & DS Code says not before 30 minutes or in the last 5 minutes. When in doubt, follow the stricter rule. (5) SIP duration: the Handbook says 4 months; the SIP Policy 2025-27 says typically 5 months, and the SIP Policy applies. (6) Capstone duration: the Handbook (AI & DS) and both programme web pages (BDA "industry project", AI & DS "capstone") say 5 months; the SIP Policy says typically 4 months, and the duration depends on the organisation. (7) Lost library books: the Late Fees Policy v02 (April 2025) prevails over the Handbook. (8) The Trim IV calendar lists a "Trim III End-Term Examination" on 1-Sep-26; students should confirm its applicability with the Program Office. The Institute may change rules at any time, and revised rules apply to the current batch.


### A.3.3 | Right to amend rules
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** All · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** Student Handbook (Disclaimer); SIP Policy §10; Admission Offer Annexure II
**Questions:** Can the Institute change the rules during my programme? | Do revised rules apply to my batch?
**Keywords:** rules change, amend, disclaimer, final decision

The Institute may change any rule in the Student Handbook from time to time, and revised rules also apply to the current batch. For SIP and placements, AIDTM may amend, modify or interpret the policy at any time, and its decisions are final and binding. Changes to fee payment dates are at the discretion of the AIDTM Admission Committee, so students should check the Institute website regularly.

# Part B — Programmes and trimester plans

The two PGDM programmes, the Executive Programme, how the two-year structure works, and the trimester-by-trimester plan for each programme with dates, exams and courses.


### B.0 | Part B at a glance: programmes and full curriculum
**Type:** overview · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS, EPAAA · **Status:** OFFICIAL-DERIVED · **Source:** Consolidated from B.2.1 to B.4.19 (Year 1 official; Year 2 allocation augmented)
**Questions:** What are all the subjects in all trimesters? | Give me the full curriculum | What will I study in each trimester? | Compare the BDA and AI & DS programmes
**Keywords:** full curriculum, all subjects all trimesters, complete course list, programme guide, what will I study each trimester, BDA and AIDS subjects

**Structure (official).** Two years, six trimesters, August 2025 to May 2027. Year 1: compulsory core courses then a Summer Internship. Year 2: electives and recent technologies, projects, seminars and the Capstone in Trimester VI. One credit = 10 contact hours. Both PGDMs include the AIHER Diploma in Strategic Management. Intake: BDA 90, AI & DS 60.

**PGDM-BDA.** Trim I (21 cr, official): Data Analysis using SQL; Express, Impress, Managers Impact; Fundamentals of Finance; Professional Communicative English-I; Python for Data Science; Statistics for Data Analytics using Excel; Project-I. Trim II (24 cr, official): Advanced Statistics; Cloud Computing; Data Exploration & Visualisation using Python; Enterprise Data Visualisation; HR Analytics; Introduction to Big Data Analytics; SIP Preparation; Project-II. Trim III (18 cr, official): Data Driven Decision Making; Introduction to Machine Learning; Python Essentials for ML; Entrepreneurship Essentials; Summer Internship (6). Trim IV (21 cr, tentative): Statistical Learning; Data Warehousing & Data Mining; NoSQL Database; Deep Learning; Marketing Management; Time Series & Forecasting; Project-IV. Trim V (21 cr, tentative): Business Analytics, Intelligence & Forecasting; Social Media & Social Network Analytics; NLP for Business; Networking, Cyber Security & Fraud Detection; Image Processing using Python; Blockchain Technology & Cyber Terrorism; Project-V. Trim VI (15 cr, tentative): Industrial Capstone Project (12); Capstone Seminar & Viva (3). Total about 120 credits.

**PGDM-AI & DS.** Trim I (24 cr, official): Data Analysis using SQL; Express, Impress, Managers Impact; Professional Communicative English-I; Introduction to AI; Management Principles; Python for Data Science; Statistics for AI & DS; Project-I. Trim II (21 cr, official): Cloud Computing; Data Exploration & Visualisation using Python; Introduction to Machine Learning; Introduction to Big Data Analytics; Image Processing using Python; SIP Preparation; Project-II. Trim III (21 cr, official): Introduction to Generative AI & Prompt Engineering; MLOps and AI Deployment; Deep Learning; Data Driven Decision Making; Project-III; Summer Internship (6). Trim IV (21 cr, tentative): Natural Language Processing; Computer Vision; Data Engineering; Gen-AI & Large Language Models; Statistical Learning; Reinforcement Learning; Project-IV. Trim V (21 cr, tentative): RPA & Intelligent Automation; Speech Recognition & Conversational AI; IoT & Edge AI; Data Intelligence and Analytics; AI Ethics, Governance & Responsible AI; Business Management; Project-V. Trim VI (15 cr, tentative): Industrial Capstone Project (12); Capstone Seminar & Viva (3). Total about 123 credits.

**Diploma in Strategic Management modules (tentative scheduling).** Trim IV: Strategic Management; Business Communication. Trim V: Competitive Advantage; AI Management; Change Management. Trim VI: Digital Transformation; Project Management.

**EPAAA (official).** Six-month executive programme from 25 July 2026, 90 hours (75 online on Saturdays, 15 on campus), INR 50,000 + 18% GST; modules: Decision Science & Analytics Thinking; Forecasting & Operational Excellence; Power BI for Decision Intelligence; AI for Enterprise; Power Apps; Power Automate & Workflow Engineering; AI Builder & Intelligent Automation.


## B.1 How the PGDM is structured


### B.1.1 | Programme duration, structure and maximum time to complete
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** Student Handbook §4.3, §11
**Questions:** How long is the PGDM? | How many trimesters are there? | When does the programme end? | What is the maximum time to complete the PGDM? | Are core courses compulsory?
**Keywords:** duration, trimesters, six terms, start date, end date, maximum years, core, elective

The PGDM runs for two years in six trimesters. The 2025-27 academic year began in August 2025 and the programme ends in May 2027. Year 1 consists of compulsory core courses, and all core courses must be passed. Year 2 focuses on electives across functional areas. Students complete a Summer Internship at the end of Year 1 and a Capstone Project in the final (sixth) trimester. A student who has not met the requirements by the end of the programme can take up to two more years, so the maximum is 2+2 = 4 years in total.


### B.1.2 | Credit system
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** AIDTM website (Course structure)
**Questions:** What is a credit? | How many hours is a 3-credit course? | How many credits is the summer internship?
**Keywords:** credit, contact hours, 3 credit course, workload, hours

One credit equals 10 in-class contact hours. A 3-credit course therefore has about 30 classroom hours and roughly 100 hours of total effort, with the rest spent on assignments, preparation and learning beyond the classroom. The Summer Internship Program carries 6 credits.


### B.1.3 | Pedagogy
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** Student Handbook §6
**Questions:** How are classes taught? | What is the teaching method at AIDTM? | Are there industrial visits and simulations?
**Keywords:** teaching method, case study, simulations, industrial visits, how classes work

AIDTM uses a student-driven blend of classroom teaching, lectures, use-case discussion and analysis, presentations, group exercises, simulations, role plays, project work, industrial visits, SIP and capstone projects, and independent self-learning. Active participation is expected, and students should come to class having read the prescribed material.


### B.1.4 | Group projects and Capstone Project
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** Student Handbook §7; AIDTM website (Placement)
**Questions:** How do group projects work? | When is the capstone project? | What happens if group members don't contribute?
**Keywords:** group project, capstone, sixth trimester, final project, team

Group projects are a major component of most courses. Groups are deliberately mixed by discipline, background and experience, and all members are expected to contribute equally; faculty may act if they do not. The Capstone Project takes place in the sixth trimester and puts students in charge of solving real-world, integrative business problems. It follows a framework of choosing a topic, doing a literature or documentation review, formulating a thesis and presenting the research. The capstone is mandatory for the award of the PGDM.


### B.1.5 | Dual degree: Diploma in Strategic Management
**Type:** profile · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** AIDTM website
**Questions:** What is the Diploma in Strategic Management? | Is the PGDM a dual degree? | Who awards the second diploma?
**Keywords:** dual degree, strategic management diploma, AIHER, second diploma

Both PGDM programmes include a Diploma in Strategic Management. It connects technical and analytical skills with business strategy, covering strategic management, competitive advantage, AI management, business communication, change management, digital transformation and project management. The diploma is awarded by the Adani Institute of Higher Education and Research (AIHER), a deemed-to-be university (NAAC A++, NIRF university rank 49).


## B.2 PGDM (Big Data Analytics)


### B.2.1 | PGDM (Big Data Analytics)
**Type:** profile · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** Student Handbook §4.1; AIDTM website
**Questions:** What is the PGDM BDA programme? | What is the intake for BDA? | What jobs can I get after BDA? | What is covered in BDA?
**Keywords:** BDA, big data analytics, programme, intake, careers, dual degree

PGDM (Big Data Analytics) is an AICTE-approved, two-year, full-time programme with an intake of 90. It is offered as a dual degree together with a Diploma in Strategic Management. Year 1 covers core management and big data analytics courses followed by the Summer Internship. Year 2 covers recent big data technologies and electives from technology and management, plus independent projects, seminars and a final industry project, which the programme page describes as 5 months long. Focus areas include machine learning, Python/R, business analytics and forecasting, enterprise data visualisation, data warehousing and mining, deep and reinforcement learning, social media analytics, time series, NLP, blockchain and cyber security. Typical roles include Data/Big Data Analyst, BI Analyst, Analytics Associate/Manager, Data Consultant, Data Manager and Big Data Engineer.


### B.2.2 | Why Big Data Analytics: market, uses and industries (BDA)
**Type:** profile · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** AIDTM website (PGDM BDA page)
**Questions:** Why study big data analytics? | What is the big data market size? | Which industries use big data analytics? | Why join AIDTM for BDA?
**Keywords:** BDA scope, market size, industries using big data, importance, why AIDTM

According to the AIDTM website, the global big data and business analytics market was US$307.51 billion in 2023 and is projected to grow from US$348.21 billion in 2024 to US$924.39 billion by 2032. Big data analytics helps organisations find hidden patterns, increase efficiency, understand markets, reduce costs, decide faster and better, and build new products. Industries that use it include e-commerce, telecom, healthcare, banking and finance, manufacturing, insurance, retail, consulting, IT, oil and gas, automobile, airlines, government, NGOs, and media and entertainment. Reasons the website gives for choosing AIDTM: an AICTE-approved programme, Adani Group leadership, industry-experienced faculty, a globally benchmarked curriculum, experts from IIMs, IITs and industry, state-of-the-art infrastructure, and corporate exposure.


### B.2.3 | PGDM-BDA full subject list (programme website)
**Type:** table · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** AIDTM website (PGDM BDA page)
**Questions:** List all BDA subjects | What subjects are in BDA second year? | Is NoSQL taught in BDA?
**Keywords:** BDA second year subjects, electives, full syllabus, NoSQL, marketing

Subjects listed for PGDM-BDA across the programme: Data Analysis using SQL; Express, Impress, Managers Impact; Fundamentals of Finance; Statistical Learning; Professional Communicative English; Python for Data Science; Statistics for Data Analytics using Excel; Cloud Computing; Data Exploration & Visualisation using Python; Enterprise Data Visualisation; HR Analytics; Introduction to Big Data Analytics; Introduction to Machine Learning; Marketing Management; Deep Learning; Image Processing using Python; Analytical Insights into Networking, Cyber Security and Fraud Detection; NoSQL Database. The exact Year 2 trimester-wise list is announced by the Program Office.


## B.3 PGDM (Artificial Intelligence & Data Science)


### B.3.1 | PGDM (Artificial Intelligence & Data Science)
**Type:** profile · **Applies to:** PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** Student Handbook §4.2; AIDTM website
**Questions:** What is the PGDM AI & DS programme? | What is the intake for AI & DS? | What careers does AI & DS lead to?
**Keywords:** AI & DS, AIDS, artificial intelligence, data science, programme, intake, careers

PGDM (AI & DS) is an AICTE-approved, two-year, full-time programme with an intake of 60. It is offered as a dual degree with a Diploma in Strategic Management. Year 1 builds foundations in AI, data science, machine learning, deep learning and predictive analytics, followed by the Summer Internship. Year 2 covers Gen-AI, RPA, NLP, data engineering and modelling, plus independent projects, seminars and a capstone project. Typical roles include Data Scientist, AI/ML Engineer, NLP Developer, Data Architect, Business Analyst (AI), AI Consultant and AIOps Engineer.


### B.3.2 | Why AI & Data Science: market, uses and industries (AI & DS)
**Type:** profile · **Applies to:** PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** AIDTM website (PGDM AI & DS page)
**Questions:** Why study AI and data science? | What is the AI market size? | Which industries use AI? | What job roles are there after AI & DS?
**Keywords:** AI DS scope, AI market size, industries using AI, importance of AI, career options

According to the AIDTM website, the AI market was estimated at US$196.63 billion in 2023 and is projected to reach US$1.87 trillion by 2032. The programme covers data analytics, machine learning applications, cloud computing, data warehousing, visualisation, deep and reinforcement learning, computer vision, NLP and generative AI. The website notes that AI helps solve complex business problems, has global impact, enables faster and automated decisions, and offers diverse career and entrepreneurial options. Industries include e-commerce, healthcare, banking and finance, manufacturing, insurance, retail, consulting, IT, telecom, oil and gas, automobile, airlines, government, NGOs, and media. Job roles listed: Data Scientist, Data Architect, Business Consultant, AI/ML Engineer, AI Solution Leader, NLP Developer, Business Analyst (AI), and Lead for Advanced Analytics & AI.


### B.3.3 | PGDM-AI & DS full subject list (programme website)
**Type:** table · **Applies to:** PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** AIDTM website (PGDM AI & DS page)
**Questions:** List all AI & DS subjects | Is reinforcement learning taught? | Is NLP or computer vision in the AI & DS syllabus?
**Keywords:** AI DS second year subjects, electives, NLP, computer vision, reinforcement learning, LLM

Subjects listed for PGDM-AI & DS across the programme: Mathematical Foundations for AI & DS; Data Science and Big Data Technologies; Foundation AI & ML; Statistical Learning; Exploratory Data Analysis; Machine Learning; Deep Learning; Gen-AI & Large Language Models; Data Engineering; Data Intelligence and Analytics; Reinforcement Learning; Natural Language Processing; Computer Vision; Internet of Things; Data Analysis Tools; Business Management; Cloud Computing Essentials; Prompt Engineering; Speech Recognition; Professional Communicative English. The exact Year 2 trimester-wise list is announced by the Program Office.


## B.4 Trimester-wise plans (dates, exams and courses)


### B.4.1 | How the Year 2 curriculum in this knowledge base was built
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Built for this project from the AIDTM website subject lists, Handbook §4 and standard PGDM practice
**Questions:** How was the Year 2 curriculum decided? | Are the second-year subjects final? | Who confirms the trimester IV-VI courses?
**Keywords:** year 2 curriculum, second year subjects, how trimester 4 5 6 courses decided, tentative

AIDTM has published a full subject list for each programme but not the trimester-wise Year 2 plan. The Trimester IV-VI course plans below place those published subjects into trimesters, following the Handbook's description of Year 2 (recent technologies, electives from management and technology, independent projects, seminars and a final capstone) and the pattern of Year 1 (3-credit courses, a project course each trimester). All Year 2 trimester allocations are tentative and are confirmed each trimester by the Program Office. The Diploma in Strategic Management modules run alongside the PGDM courses.

AI & DS Trimester I courses, with short name, faculty and sessions: Data Analysis using SQL (DASQL), Mr. Vyankatesh Emche, 22 sessions, plus a DASQL Lab, Mr. Emche, 9 sessions. Express, Impress, Managers Impact (EIMI), visiting faculty Mr. Vishal Shah, 20 sessions. Professional Communicative English - I (PCE-I), Dr. Roshni Rawal, 24 sessions. Introduction to AI (IAI), shared by Dr. Bhavesh Dharmani (18 sessions) and Dr. Ankit Shah (14 sessions), plus an IAI Lab, Dr. Dharmani, 15 sessions. Python for Data Science (PDS), Dr. Ankit Shah, 25 sessions, plus a PDS Lab, Mr. Emche, 8 sessions. Statistics for AI & DS (SAI&DS), visiting faculty Dr. Nisarg Joshi, 24 sessions, plus a SAI&DS Lab, also Dr. Joshi, 10 sessions. Project - I has no faculty listed on the sheet, which also has a line for expert sessions.


### B.4.15 | AI & DS Trimester I session plan totals
**Type:** table · **Applies to:** PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** OFFICIAL-DERIVED · **Source:** Derived from AI & DS Trimester I course-faculty sheet
**Questions:** How many sessions are there in AI & DS Trimester I? | How many lab sessions are there? | How many sessions does each faculty teach?
**Keywords:** total sessions, lab sessions, theory sessions, number of classes, workload trim 1

Theory sessions: DASQL 22, EIMI 20, PCE-I 24, IAI 32 (18 by Dr. Bhavesh Dharmani and 14 by Dr. Ankit Shah), PDS 25 and SAI&DS 24, for 147 in total. Lab sessions: DASQL Lab 9, IAI Lab 15, PDS Lab 8 and SAI&DS Lab 10, for 42 in total. Grand total: 189 scheduled sessions, plus expert sessions. Sessions per faculty member: Mr. Vyankatesh Emche 39 (DASQL 22, DASQL Lab 9, PDS Lab 8); Dr. Ankit Shah 39 (IAI 14, PDS 25); Dr. Bhavesh Dharmani 33 (IAI 18, IAI Lab 15); Dr. Nisarg Joshi 34 (SAI&DS 24 + Lab 10); Dr. Roshni Rawal 24; Mr. Vishal Shah 20. Management Principles (3 credits in the Handbook) and Project - I have no faculty or sessions on the sheet.


### B.4.16 | Subjects for each programme: master list for all three programmes
**Type:** table · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS, EPAAA · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** Handbook Annexures I A and I B; AIDTM website programme pages
**Questions:** List the subjects of all three programmes | What are the EPAAA modules? | What are all Year 1 subjects for BDA and AI & DS?
**Keywords:** subjects per programme, all subjects, BDA subjects, AI DS subjects, executive program modules, three programmes, curriculum

AIDTM offers three programmes: PGDM (Big Data Analytics), PGDM (AI & Data Science) and the Executive Program in AI, Analytics & Automation (EPAAA).
PGDM-BDA Year 1, 63 credits. Trim I (21): Data Analysis using SQL; Express, Impress, Managers Impact; Fundamentals of Finance; Professional Communicative English - I; Python for Data Science; Statistics for Data Analytics using Excel; Project - I. Trim II (24): Advanced Statistics; Cloud Computing; Data Exploration & Visualisation using Python; Enterprise Data Visualisation; HR Analytics; Introduction to Big Data Analytics; SIP Preparation; Project - II. Trim III (18): Data Driven Decision Making; Introduction to Machine Learning; Python Essentials for Machine Learning; Entrepreneurship Essentials; Summer Internship Program (6 credits).
PGDM-AI & DS Year 1, 66 credits. Trim I (24): Data Analysis using SQL; Express, Impress, Managers Impact; Professional Communicative English - I; Introduction to AI; Management Principles; Python for Data Science; Statistics for AI & DS; Project - I. Trim II (21): Cloud Computing; Data Exploration & Visualisation using Python; Introduction to Machine Learning; Introduction to Big Data Analytics; Image Processing using Python; SIP Preparation; Project - II. Trim III (21): Introduction to Generative AI & Prompt Engineering; MLOps and AI Deployment; Deep Learning; Data Driven Decision Making; Project - III; Summer Internship Program (6 credits).
EPAAA modules: Decision Science & Analytics Thinking; Forecasting & Operational Excellence; Power BI for Decision Intelligence; AI for Enterprise; Power Apps (Low-Code Enterprise Solutions); Power Automate & Workflow Engineering; AI Builder & Intelligent Automation.
Year 2 PGDM subjects are listed in B.2.3 (BDA) and B.3.3 (AI & DS); a tentative trimester-wise Year 2 plan (AUGMENTED) is in B.4.5 to B.4.13.


### B.4.17 | BDA vs AI & DS: common and programme-specific Year 1 courses
**Type:** table · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** OFFICIAL-DERIVED · **Source:** Derived from Handbook Annexures I A and I B
**Questions:** What is the difference between BDA and AI & DS? | Which subjects are common to both programmes? | Which subjects are only in BDA or only in AI & DS? | How many credits is Year 1?
**Keywords:** difference between BDA and AI DS, common subjects, unique subjects, compare programmes

Courses common to both programmes in Year 1: Data Analysis using SQL; Express, Impress, Managers Impact; Professional Communicative English - I; Python for Data Science; Project - I; Cloud Computing; Data Exploration & Visualisation using Python; Introduction to Big Data Analytics; SIP Preparation; Project - II; Introduction to Machine Learning (BDA in Trim III, AI & DS in Trim II); Data Driven Decision Making; Summer Internship Program. Only in BDA: Fundamentals of Finance; Statistics for Data Analytics using Excel; Advanced Statistics; Enterprise Data Visualisation; HR Analytics; Python Essentials for Machine Learning; Entrepreneurship Essentials. Only in AI & DS: Introduction to AI; Management Principles; Statistics for AI & DS; Image Processing using Python; Introduction to Generative AI & Prompt Engineering; MLOps and AI Deployment; Deep Learning; Project - III. Year 1 credits: BDA 63, AI & DS 66. Intake: BDA 90, AI & DS 60. BDA leans towards business analytics and management, AI & DS towards AI/ML engineering and Gen-AI.


### B.4.18 | Diploma in Strategic Management: module schedule
**Type:** table · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Module names from the AIDTM website; scheduling built for this project
**Questions:** When are the strategic management diploma classes? | Which diploma modules are in which trimester?
**Keywords:** strategic management diploma subjects, AIHER diploma modules, when are diploma classes

The seven Diploma in Strategic Management modules are spread across Year 2 and are usually taught in intensive weekend or evening blocks by AIHER and AIDTM faculty. Trimester IV: Strategic Management; Business Communication. Trimester V: Competitive Advantage; AI Management; Change Management. Trimester VI: Digital Transformation; Project Management. Each module is assessed by a case-based assignment and a short exam, and the diploma is awarded by AIHER together with the PGDM, subject to passing all seven modules.


### B.4.19 | Year 2 electives: choice and minimum enrolment
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Built for this project
**Questions:** How do electives work? | Can I take an elective from the other programme? | What is the minimum number of students for an elective?
**Keywords:** electives, choose elective, elective basket, minimum students, cross programme elective

Year 2 management electives are offered from a common basket for both programmes: Marketing Management, Business Management, HR Analytics (for AI & DS students who did not take it), Fundamentals of Finance (for AI & DS students), Entrepreneurship Essentials (for AI & DS students) and Digital Marketing Analytics. Students choose one management elective in Trimester IV and one in Trimester V through the elective-selection form issued by the Program Office two weeks before the trimester. An elective runs only if at least 15 students choose it, and changes follow D.4.1 (first week of the trimester only). Students can also take one elective from the other programme's basket with Programme Chair approval, subject to seats.


## B.5 Executive Program in AI, Analytics & Automation (EPAAA)


### B.5.1 | Executive Program in AI, Analytics & Automation (EPAAA)
**Type:** profile · **Applies to:** EPAAA · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** AIDTM website (EPAAA page)
**Questions:** What is EPAAA? | What is the executive programme at AIDTM? | Who can apply to EPAAA? | What is the EPAAA fee?
**Keywords:** executive program, working professionals, EPAAA, Power BI, weekend, fee 50000, GST

EPAAA is a six-month programme for working graduates. It begins on 25 July 2026 and has 90 learning hours: 75 hours online (25 Saturday sessions of 3 hours) plus 15 hours of on-campus immersion over 2 days. Fee: INR 50,000 + 18% GST (INR 59,000 in total). Modules: Decision Science & Analytics Thinking; Forecasting & Operational Excellence; Power BI for Decision Intelligence; AI for Enterprise; Power Apps; Power Automate & Workflow Engineering; AI Builder & Intelligent Automation. Tools covered include Excel, Power BI, Power Apps, Power Automate, AI Builder and Microsoft Copilot. Eligibility: a bachelor's or master's degree in any discipline, with no programming required. To apply, fill in the registration form and pay to confirm. Contact: Mr. Alok Giri, 8851223902, exedu@aidtm.ac.in. A certificate is awarded once the programme requirements are met. PGDM rules in this knowledge base do not apply to EPAAA unless stated.


### B.5.2 | EPAAA at a glance
**Type:** table · **Applies to:** EPAAA · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** AIDTM website (EPAAA page)
**Questions:** When does EPAAA start? | How many hours and sessions is EPAAA? | How do I apply to the executive programme? | Who do I contact for EPAAA?
**Keywords:** executive program details, EPAAA duration, start date, online sessions, campus immersion, fees, eligibility, how to apply

| Item | Details |
|---|---|
| Programme duration | 6 months |
| Programme begins | 25 July 2026 |
| Learning hours | 90 |
| Online classes | 75 hours (Saturday, 3 hours a week) |
| Campus immersion | 15 hours (2 days) |
| Number of online sessions | 25 |
| Who should attend | Working professionals after graduation |
| Programme fee | INR 50,000 + 18% GST, which is INR 9,000 (total INR 59,000) |

Eligibility: a bachelor's or master's degree in any discipline, with no programming skills required. To apply: fill in the registration form, wait for AIDTM to contact you, then pay the fee to confirm enrolment. Contact: Mr. Alok Giri, 8851223902, exedu@aidtm.ac.in. Certificate: awarded after meeting the requirements and policies set by AIDTM. Delivery is on weekends: online sessions plus on-campus immersion.


### B.5.3 | EPAAA tools and learning outcomes
**Type:** profile · **Applies to:** EPAAA · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** AIDTM website (EPAAA page)
**Questions:** What will I learn in EPAAA? | What tools does EPAAA cover? | What are the EPAAA learning outcomes?
**Keywords:** EPAAA outcomes, Power Automate, Copilot, AI Builder, what will I learn executive program

EPAAA tools: Excel for analysis and modelling; Power BI for dashboards and business intelligence; Power Apps for low-code applications; Power Automate for workflow automation; AI Builder for no-code AI (prediction, form processing, text recognition); Microsoft Copilot for Gen-AI assistance. Learning outcomes: analyse business problems with data-driven frameworks; apply statistics and forecasting; design KPI frameworks; build interactive dashboards; apply no-code AI tools; evaluate process-optimisation opportunities; build low-code apps; automate workflows; integrate AI into apps and workflows; and evaluate the ethics, governance and risk of deploying AI. Highlights include an industry-designed curriculum, quizzes and assignments, experts from the Adani Group and around the world, and a project using synthetic or the participant's own business data.

## C.1 Courses common to both PGDM programmes


### C.1.1 | Data Analysis using SQL (DASQL)
**Type:** course · **Applies to:** PGDM-AIDS, PGDM-BDA · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Student Handbook Annexure I (name, trimester, credits for Year 1); representative syllabus for this helpdesk
**Questions:** What is taught in Data Analysis using SQL? | What are the units or topics of Data Analysis using SQL (DASQL)? | Which trimester is Data Analysis using SQL in? | How many credits is Data Analysis using SQL? | Who teaches Data Analysis using SQL? | How is Data Analysis using SQL assessed?
**Keywords:** Data Analysis using SQL, DASQL, syllabus, course content, units, topics

| Item | Detail |
|---|---|
| Offered in | PGDM (Big Data Analytics): Trimester I (3 credits); PGDM (AI & Data Science): Trimester I (3 credits) |
| Short name | DASQL |
| Faculty | AI & DS: Mr. Vyankatesh Emche (22 theory + 9 lab sessions) |
| Status | Name, trimester and credits are official (Student Handbook Annexure I); the syllabus below is representative |

**What the course covers.** Relational databases and SQL for business analysis: querying, joining and aggregating data to answer management questions.

**Units**

1. Relational model, ER diagrams, keys and normalisation
2. SELECT, filtering, sorting and built-in functions
3. Joins, subqueries and set operations
4. GROUP BY, window functions and common table expressions
5. Views, indexes, transactions and a reporting mini-project

**Learning outcomes**

- Write analytical SQL on multi-table data
- Design a normalised schema
- Build KPI reports directly from a database

**Typical assessment.** Mid-term exam 25% · End-term exam 35% · Lab work and assignments 25% · Quizzes and class participation 15%. The binding scheme is the one in the faculty's course outline (D.2.1).

**Tools.** MySQL / PostgreSQL, MySQL Workbench

**Type:** course · **Applies to:** PGDM-AIDS, PGDM-BDA · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Student Handbook Annexure I (name, trimester, credits for Year 1); representative syllabus for this helpdesk

**What the course covers.** The statistical foundations of predictive modelling: inference, resampling, model selection and non-linear methods.

## C.4 Projects, Summer Internship and Capstone


### C.4.1 | Project courses I to V (PRJ)
**Type:** course · **Applies to:** PGDM-AIDS, PGDM-BDA · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Student Handbook Annexure I (name, trimester, credits for Year 1); representative syllabus for this helpdesk
**Questions:** What is taught in Project courses I to V? | What are the units or topics of Project courses I to V (PRJ)? | Which trimester is Project courses I to V in? | How many credits is Project courses I to V? | Who teaches Project courses I to V? | How is Project courses I to V assessed?
**Keywords:** Project courses I to V, PRJ, syllabus, course content, units, topics

| Item | Detail |
|---|---|
| Offered in | PGDM (Big Data Analytics): Trimester I (3 credits); PGDM (Big Data Analytics): Trimester II (3 credits); PGDM (Big Data Analytics): Trimester IV (3 credits); PGDM (Big Data Analytics): Trimester V (3 credits); PGDM (AI & Data Science): Trimester I (3 credits); PGDM (AI & Data Science): Trimester II (3 credits); PGDM (AI & Data Science): Trimester III (3 credits); PGDM (AI & Data Science): Trimester IV (3 credits); PGDM (AI & Data Science): Trimester V (3 credits) |
| Short name | PRJ |
| Faculty | A faculty guide is assigned to each group |
| Status | Trimester placement and syllabus are a representative helpdesk plan; confirm with the Program Office |

**What the course covers.** A project in each trimester, done in groups of 3-4, that applies that trimester's courses to a real dataset or business problem. BDA has Projects I, II, IV and V; AI & DS has Projects I to V.

**Units**

1. Project I: exploratory analysis of a public business dataset (SQL, Python, statistics)
2. Project II: visualisation / big-data / cloud project
3. Project III (AI & DS): GenAI or MLOps deployment project
4. Project IV: applied ML or deep-learning project in a business domain
5. Project V: capstone proposal and pre-work

**Learning outcomes**

- Work in a team on an end-to-end analytics problem
- Present technical work to non-technical audiences
- Build a portfolio for placements

**Typical assessment.** Proposal 15% · Mid-review 25% · Final report 35% · Presentation and demo 25%. The binding scheme is the one in the faculty's course outline (D.2.1).

**Tools.** Course tools, Git

**Typical assessment.** Proposal 15% · Mid-review 25% · Final report 35% · Presentation and demo 25%. The binding scheme is the one in the faculty's course outline (D.2.1).

**Tools.** LMS, presentation tools

# Part D — Academic regulations

Attendance and leave, evaluation and grading, appeals and failing a course, graduation criteria, course changes, records and responsible use of AI tools.


### D.0 | Part D at a glance: attendance, grading, appeals and graduation
**Type:** overview · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** OFFICIAL-DERIVED · **Source:** Consolidated from D.1.1 to D.4.5, D.3.2 (Student Handbook §10-11, Annexures III-V; re-assessment fees are augmented)
**Questions:** Explain all the academic rules | How am I evaluated and graded? | What are the pass and fail rules? | Give me the complete grading and attendance policy
**Keywords:** everything about grading, academic rules guide, attendance and grades explained, how am I evaluated, pass fail rules

**1. Attendance (official).** 100% is expected in this fully residential programme. With prior permission and a genuine reason, absence is allowed up to 30% of sessions. Below 70% in a course, including medical absence, a student cannot sit that course's end-term (the Institute may make exceptions). No make-up for missed quizzes, exams or presentations. Late arrival earns no attendance. Attendance does not earn class-participation marks. Absence can also attract a monetary penalty.

**2. Evaluation (official).** Continuous evaluation with components such as mid-term, end-term, cases, class participation, assignments, projects, quizzes and tests, announced in the course outline.

**3. Grades (official).** A (Excellent) 3.55-4.33; B (Good) 2.55-3.54; C (Satisfactory) 1.55-2.54; D (Low Pass) 0.55-1.54; F (Fail) below 0.55; I (Incomplete). Component marks become letter grades, then grade points; the sum of component values divided by 3 gives the course grade point and final letter grade. Maximum CGPA 4.33. GPA = Σ(credits × grade points) / Σ credits; CGPA weights trimester GPAs by trimester credits. Percentage = CGPA / 4.33 × 100; 3.04 = 70%, 2.60 = 60%, 2.17 = 50%.

**4. I grade (official).** Temporary; first-year I grades must be cleared before the Summer Internship, second-year ones within the following term; uncleared I becomes F.

**5. Disputes and appeals (official).** Discuss with the faculty within 4 days (Handbook) or appeal in writing within a week (Annexure V); faculty replies within a week; then appeal to the Programme Chair within a week with evidence; the Academic Committee meets within 15 days and the decision is communicated within 2 days; a final appeal lies to the Dean.

**6. Failing a course (helpdesk guidance; the Academic Committee's role is official).** Apply for re-assessment within 10 days of results; the Committee decides eligibility after a one-on-one; supplementary exam in the first month of the next trimester (INR 2,500, best grade C); otherwise repeat the course when next offered (INR 5,000).

**7. Graduation (official).** Satisfactory capstone, CGPA at least 2.17 at the end of Year 2, no F or I in any course, no dues, SIP and capstone completed, all within course duration + 2 years (maximum 4 years). Students who fall short are asked to withdraw.


## D.1 Attendance, leave and class timings


### D.1.1 | Attendance policy
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** Student Handbook §10.2, Annexure III
**Questions:** What is the minimum attendance? | Can I sit the end-term exam with less than 70% attendance? | How many classes can I miss? | Do I get attendance if I am late? | Is there a make-up exam if I miss one?
**Keywords:** attendance, minimum attendance, 70 percent, absent, late coming, medical absence, residential

PGDM is a fully residential programme, and 100% attendance is expected. For a genuine reason and with prior permission, a student may be absent for at most 30% of total sessions. A student below 70% attendance in a course, including absence for medical reasons, is not eligible for that course's End-Term Examination, though the Institute may allow exceptions. Missed quizzes, examinations or presentations cannot be made up. Arriving late to class earns no attendance. Attendance is separate from class participation, so being present does not by itself earn class participation grades. The Class Representative coordinates seating and attendance matters with faculty. Absence from classes can attract disciplinary action, including a monetary penalty.


### D.1.2 | Leave of absence: types and how to apply
**Type:** procedure · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Helpdesk policy written for this project (not issued by AIDTM); attendance limits from Student Handbook Annexure III (official)
**Questions:** How do I apply for leave? | What do I do if I am sick? | Does medical leave count as absence? | Can I take leave for a family emergency? | What happens if I am absent for a long time?
**Keywords:** leave application, sick leave, medical leave, absent permission, emergency leave, duty leave

**Leave types and procedure**

| Leave type | How to apply | Documents | Approved by |
|---|---|---|---|
| Planned personal leave | Email the Program Office at least 2 working days before, with dates, reason and the courses affected | None, unless asked | Programme Officer |
| Medical leave | Inform the Program Office (9099981586) on the first day of illness | Doctor's certificate within 3 days of returning; fitness certificate after hospitalisation of more than 7 days | Programme Officer |
| Family emergency or bereavement | Inform the Program Office as soon as possible | Supporting document within one week | Programme Officer |
| Institute duty (placement interview, official competition, Institute event) | Request through the Program Office before the activity | Invitation or schedule | Programme Chair |

**Rules that always apply (official, Student Handbook Annexure III):**

- Any absence, including medical, counts towards the 30% absence limit.
- Below 70% attendance in a course means no end-term examination in that course.
- Missed quizzes, examinations or presentations cannot be made up. The exception is a component missed with prior permission, where an I grade and a make-up apply (D.2.4).
- Arriving late to a class earns no attendance for that class.

**Other points:**

- Hostel residents who will be away overnight also need the warden's approval (L.1.3).
- Approved Institute-duty leave is marked present only when the activity was official and approved in advance.
- Absence of more than 3 weeks in a trimester is referred to the Academic Committee, which may advise repeating that trimester's courses with the next batch.


### D.1.3 | Class timings and weekly schedule
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Sample schedule built for this project (library hours are official)
**Questions:** What are the class timings? | How long is each session? | Are there classes on Saturday? | When is the lunch break? | When is the timetable published?
**Keywords:** class timings, session duration, how many classes a day, Saturday classes, lunch break

Classes run Monday to Friday from 9:30 AM to 5:30 PM, with up to four 90-minute sessions a day (9:30-11:00, 11:15-12:45, 1:45-3:15, 3:30-5:00) and a lunch break from 12:45 to 1:45 PM. Saturday mornings (9:30 AM to 1:30 PM) are used for visiting-faculty sessions, labs, expert sessions and make-up classes. The weekly timetable is published on the portal every Friday for the following week. Late arrival after the session starts earns no attendance.


## D.2 Evaluation and grading


### D.2.1 | Evaluation components
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** Student Handbook §10.1
**Questions:** How am I evaluated in a course? | What are the evaluation components? | Where do I find the marks breakdown for a course?
**Keywords:** evaluation, assessment, marks components, continuous evaluation, course outline

AIDTM follows continuous evaluation. Each course combines components such as a mid-term exam, an end-term exam, case discussions, class participation, assignments, projects, quizzes and class tests. The faculty member announces the components and their weights in the course outline at the start of the course.


### D.2.2 | Grading scale
**Type:** table · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** Student Handbook §10.3, Annexure IV
**Questions:** What is the grading scale? | What grade points does an A get? | What is the pass grade? | What is the maximum CGPA? | How is the final course grade calculated?
**Keywords:** grade, grade points, A B C D F, pass marks, fail, grading system, 4.33

AIDTM uses a five-point letter scale: A (Excellent) is 3.55-4.33; B (Good) is 2.55-3.54; C (Satisfactory) is 1.55-2.54; D (Low Pass) is 0.55-1.54; F (Fail) is below 0.55; I means Incomplete, with the final grade pending. Marks for each component are converted to letter grades, with the instructor setting the ranges, and the component letter grades are then converted to numerical values (up to two decimal points) using the Grade Conversion Table. The component values are added and divided by 3 to get the course grade point, which is converted to the final letter grade. The maximum CGPA is 4.33, and the medium of instruction is English.


### D.2.3 | GPA, CGPA and percentage equivalence
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** Student Handbook §10.4-10.5, Annexure IV
**Questions:** How is GPA calculated? | How is CGPA calculated? | How do I convert CGPA to percentage? | What CGPA is first class or 60%?
**Keywords:** GPA, CGPA, calculate GPA, percentage conversion, first class, division

Trimester GPA = Σ(Ci × Pi) / Σ Ci, where Ci is the credits of course i and Pi is the grade point earned in it. CGPA = Σ(Ci × Gi) / Σ Ci over trimesters, where Ci is the total credits of trimester i and Gi is that trimester's GPA. To convert to a percentage: Percentage = (CGPA obtained / 4.33) × 100. Equivalent classes: a CGPA of 3.04 or above corresponds to 70% or above, 2.60 or above to 60% or above, and 2.17 or above to 50% or above.


### D.2.4 | The "I" (Incomplete) grade and missed components
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** Student Handbook §10.3.1
**Questions:** What is an I grade? | What happens if I miss an exam with permission? | When must I clear an incomplete grade? | Does an I grade become F?
**Keywords:** incomplete grade, I grade, missed exam with permission, make-up, converted to F

An I grade is a temporary grade given when a course's requirements are not complete, for example when a component was missed with prior permission. First-year students must clear any I grade before going on their Summer Internship. Second-year students must clear it within the following term, and a sixth-term I must be cleared in time to graduate. If the make-up requirements set by the instructor are completed on time, the I is replaced by the earned grade. Otherwise the missed component gets an F, and an uncleared I becomes an F. Note: under the attendance policy there is no make-up for components missed without permission.


### D.2.5 | How grades are communicated
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** Student Handbook §10.6
**Questions:** When will I get my grades? | Who communicates the final grades? | How are marks shared?
**Keywords:** results, grade sheet, when will I get grades, marks communication

Instructors share component marks, from quizzes and exams, with students as soon as possible. The Program Office communicates final letter grades for each course, along with GPA and CGPA. A student can see only their own grades.


## D.3 Appeals, failing a course and graduation


### D.3.1 | Grade dispute and revision appeal process
**Type:** procedure · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** Student Handbook §10.3.1 (IV-V), §10.7, Annexure V
**Questions:** How do I appeal a grade? | I am not happy with my marks, what can I do? | What is the grade revision process? | How long do I have to dispute a grade?
**Keywords:** grade appeal, revaluation, recheck, dispute marks, grade revision, unhappy with grade

Step 1: discuss the grade with the faculty member. The Handbook asks for this within 4 days of receiving grades, and Annexure V allows a written appeal within one week of the announcement, with reasons. The faculty member replies in writing within one week. Step 2: if still unsatisfied, appeal in writing to the Programme Chair within one week of the faculty decision. Include reasons, evidence, copies of the original appeal and the faculty reply, and other documents. Step 3: the Programme Chair convenes the Academic Committee within 15 days, and the decision is communicated within 2 days. Step 4: a student can appeal the Academic Committee's decision to the Dean, whose decision is final and binding. For the Term-III end-term exam, queries can be sent to faculty by email.


### D.3.2 | Failing a course: re-assessment, supplementary exam and repeat
**Type:** procedure · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Sample procedure built for this project; the Academic Committee's role in re-assessment eligibility is official (Handbook §16.1)
**Questions:** What happens if I fail a subject? | Is there a supplementary exam? | How do I clear an F grade? | What is the re-assessment fee?
**Keywords:** failed a subject, F grade, supplementary exam, repeat course, re-assessment, clear backlog

A student with an F grade applies for re-assessment to the Academic Committee through the Program Office within 10 days of results. The Committee holds a one-on-one interaction and decides eligibility (official role). If eligible, the student sits a supplementary examination for the end-term component in the first month of the next trimester, paying INR 2,500. The best grade obtainable through a supplementary exam is C. If the supplementary is failed or the student is not eligible, the course must be repeated when next offered, with a fee of INR 5,000, and the repeat grade replaces the F. First-year F grades must be cleared before the Summer Internship, and all F grades must be cleared within the programme's maximum duration of 4 years (official).


### D.3.3 | Criteria for award of the PGDM
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** Student Handbook §11
**Questions:** What do I need to graduate? | What is the minimum CGPA to get the PGDM? | Can I graduate with an F grade? | Can I get my diploma if I have dues?
**Keywords:** graduation requirements, pass criteria, minimum CGPA 2.17, diploma award, eligibility to graduate

To receive the PGDM, a student must have completed the capstone project satisfactorily, have a CGPA of at least 2.17 at the end of Year 2, have no F or I grade in any course, and have no outstanding dues to the Institute. SIP and the Industrial Capstone Project are also mandatory. A student who does not meet these criteria will be asked to withdraw. All requirements must be completed within the course duration plus 2 years.


## D.4 Course changes, records and student systems


### D.4.1 | Course changes, electives and programme switch
**Type:** procedure · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Helpdesk policy written for this project (not issued by AIDTM); core courses compulsory per Student Handbook §4.3 (official)
**Questions:** Can I change my course or elective? | How do I drop an elective? | Can I switch from AI & DS to BDA? | Can I audit a course? | What is the deadline to change electives?
**Keywords:** change course, change elective, drop course, add course, switch programme, audit course, transfer

| Change | Allowed? | Window | How to request | Approved by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Drop or swap a Year 1 core course | No (core courses are compulsory, official) | — | — | — |
| Add or drop a Year 2 elective | Yes | First week of the trimester (until Friday of week 1) | "Course Change" support request or the elective form | Program Office; Programme Chair if there is a timetable clash |
| Take one elective from the other programme's basket | Yes, at most one per trimester | Elective selection, 2 weeks before the trimester | Request with reason | Programme Chair |
| Audit a course (attend without credit) | Yes, with the faculty member's consent | First two weeks | Email the faculty and the Program Office | Faculty |
| Switch programme (BDA to AI & DS or back) | Only before Trimester I ends | — | Written application; treated as a sensitive change (N.1.2) | Academic Committee |
| Repeat a failed course | When the course is next offered | After results | See D.3.2 | Academic Committee |

**Conditions:**

- An elective runs only if at least 15 students choose it, and seats and timetable clashes are checked before approval.
- Approved changes appear on the student portal within 2 working days.
- Withdrawing from an elective after week 1 is recorded as F, unless the Academic Committee approves it for medical reasons.

**Programme switch:**

- The Academic Committee considers the reason, Trimester I performance and seat availability (intake: BDA 90, AI & DS 60).
- Trimester I courses that are not common to both programmes must be completed later. For a switch to AI & DS these are Introduction to AI, Statistics for AI & DS and Management Principles. For a switch to BDA they are Fundamentals of Finance and Statistics for Data Analytics using Excel.
- The fee is unchanged, because both programmes have the same fee structure.
- The switch takes effect only after approval. If it is rejected, the registration stays as it was.

### D.4.3 | Student portal and LMS
**Type:** reference · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Sample description built for this project

The student portal shows the timetable, attendance percentage per course, component marks and grades, fee status and receipts, scholarship status and support requests. The LMS hosts course outlines, reading material, assignment submissions with plagiarism checking, quizzes and feedback forms. Attendance recorded by the classroom face-recognition devices is synced to the portal daily. Discrepancies must be reported within 7 days through an "Academics, Grades & Attendance" request.


### D.4.4 | Laptop and software requirements
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Sample requirement built for this project
**Questions:** Do I need a laptop? | What laptop configuration is required? | What software do I need to install?
**Keywords:** laptop required, system requirements, software needed, Python, Power BI, Anaconda

Every student must have a laptop with at least an i5 or equivalent processor, 16 GB RAM, 512 GB SSD and Windows 11 or macOS. Software used across courses: Python with Anaconda and Jupyter, VS Code, Git, MySQL or PostgreSQL, Power BI Desktop, Tableau Public, R and RStudio, Microsoft 365 (provided through the Institute account) and a cloud sandbox account (AWS, Azure or Google Cloud free tier) for the Cloud Computing course. Installation support is available from the IT Officer in the first two weeks of Trimester I.


### D.4.5 | Student course feedback
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** Student Handbook §8
**Questions:** How do I give feedback on a course? | How often is course feedback collected?
**Keywords:** feedback form, course feedback, faculty feedback

Students give feedback on their courses twice per term. The Program Office circulates the questionnaire, and when it is online it must be submitted on the portal within the stated time window. Feedback is used to improve course quality.


## D.5 Responsible use of generative AI tools


### D.5.1 | Using ChatGPT and other AI tools in coursework
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Helpdesk policy written for this project (not issued by AIDTM); plagiarism and unfair-means rules it relies on are official (Student Handbook §12)
**Questions:** Can I use ChatGPT for assignments? | Is using AI tools plagiarism? | Do I have to declare AI use? | Can I upload company data to an AI tool? | What happens if I use AI in an exam?
**Keywords:** ChatGPT, generative AI, AI tools, AI declaration, AI plagiarism, LLM use in assignments

AIDTM encourages students to learn with AI tools, especially in the AI & DS programme, within the rules below.

**Allowed by default:** brainstorming, having concepts explained, debugging your own code and checking grammar.

**Must be declared:** any AI-generated text, code, images or analysis in submitted work. Add a short "AI use statement" naming the tool, what you used it for and what you changed.

**Not allowed unless the faculty explicitly permits it:**

- Submitting AI-generated answers as your own work in graded assignments, take-home exams or reports.
- Using any AI tool in a closed-book examination. This is unfair means (E.2.1).
- Uploading confidential case data, SIP or capstone company data, or other students' work to public AI tools. This breaches data confidentiality (J.2.2).

**Levels set by faculty for each assignment:**

| Level | Meaning |
|---|---|
| 0 | No AI use |
| 1 | AI for ideas only |
| 2 | AI assistance, with a declaration |
| 3 | AI as an integral tool of the task |

**Enforcement:**

- Undeclared AI use is treated as plagiarism (J.1.2), with the same penalties, from a penalty grade up to Disciplinary Committee action.
- Similarity and AI-detection checks may be run on submissions. A detector score alone is not proof: the student can explain their process to the faculty member before any decision.

# Part E — Examinations

What to bring, when to arrive, what is prohibited, how to behave in the hall, when you may leave, and how cheating is handled. Exam dates for every trimester are in E.0 and Part H.


### E.0 | Part E at a glance: exam rules and dates
**Type:** overview · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** OFFICIAL-DERIVED · **Source:** Consolidated from E.1.1 to E.3.2 (Student Handbook §9, §12.1; AI & DS Examination Code of Conduct)
**Questions:** Tell me everything about exams | What are the exam rules? | What should I bring to the exam? | What are the exam dates for all trimesters?
**Keywords:** everything about exams, exam rules guide, exam day instructions, what to bring to exam, exam do's and don'ts

**Before the exam.** AI & DS students: be seated 15 minutes before the start; no entry after it begins; bring the Institute ID card and hall ticket or entry is refused. Handbook rule (BDA): be seated 10 minutes before; late entry at the coordinator's discretion, never after 30 minutes. Wear the ID card at all times; not carrying it in exam hours costs INR 100, and a replacement hall ticket costs INR 100. Clear any fee dues: unpaid fees beyond 6 weeks bar you from exams.

**What you may and may not bring.** Allowed: your own basic calculator (no borrowing or lending), and for open-book exams only the material the faculty specifies. Not allowed: phones, smart watches, iPads, Bluetooth and electronic devices, books, notes, papers and scientific calculators. Phones are switched off and placed on the main table; a phone in a pocket, even switched off, breaks the rule and is confiscated (return fine INR 2,000 first time, INR 5,000 second time, permanent confiscation third time, and the Institute may inspect the data).

**During the exam.** Sit in the allotted seat; write the seat number and particulars on every answer book and supplement and get them signed by the supervisor; write legibly; rough work only on the last page of the answer book, never on the question paper; one student at a time to the restroom with permission; no talking, signalling or exchange of papers or materials; looking at another's answer book counts as copying; exams are on CCTV.

**Leaving.** AI & DS Code: not before 30 minutes and not in the last 5 minutes. Handbook: not in the first half hour or the last 15 minutes; early finishers hand in to the supervisor. Everyone stays until all answer books are collected, and answer books never leave the room.

**Unfair means.** Minor attempts: on-the-spot warning, seat change or mark deduction. Serious attempts: written statement in another room, answer book seized, continue on a fresh book without extra time, report to the Program Office, referral by the Programme Chair to the Disciplinary Committee; penalties up to expulsion with a mercy appeal to the Dean.

**Exam dates for Batch 2025-27.** Trim I mid-terms 15-18 Sep 2025, end-terms 7-11 Oct 2025; Trim II mid-terms 1-3 Dec 2025, end-terms 1-6 Jan 2026; Trim III mid-terms 19-21 Feb 2026, end-terms 23-28 Mar 2026; Trim IV mid-terms 5-6 Oct 2026, end-term 31 Oct 2026; Trim V (tentative) mid-terms 21-23 Dec 2026, end-terms 1-5 Feb 2027; Trim VI capstone review 5-6 Apr 2027 and viva 17-21 May 2027 (tentative).


## E.1 Exam day


### E.1.1 | Reporting time, entry and identity documents
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-AIDS (Code of Conduct); PGDM-BDA (Handbook) · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** AI & DS Examination Code of Conduct (1, 3, 11); Student Handbook §9
**Questions:** What time should I reach the exam hall? | Can I enter the exam late? | What documents do I need for the exam? | What if I forget my ID card or hall ticket?
**Keywords:** exam reporting time, late for exam, hall ticket, ID card, entry to exam hall

AI & DS students must be at their exam seat 15 minutes before the start, and no entry is allowed after the exam begins. They must bring both the Institute ID card and the hall ticket, without which entry is refused. Not carrying the I-Card during exam hours is fined INR 100, and a replacement for a lost hall ticket costs INR 100. Handbook rule, which applies to BDA: be seated 10 minutes before the start. Entry after the start is at the examination coordinator's discretion, but never after the first 30 minutes. Students must wear their ID and produce it when asked, and must sit in their allotted seat.


### E.1.2 | Items not allowed in the examination hall
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** Exam Code of Conduct (2); Student Handbook §9 (6, 8); Annexure VII
**Questions:** Can I bring my phone to the exam? | Is a scientific calculator allowed? | Can I bring notes to the exam? | What happens if my phone is found in the exam?
**Keywords:** calculator, mobile in exam, smart watch, bluetooth, notes allowed, open book

Prohibited items: all electronic and Bluetooth devices, mobile phones, smart watches, iPads, books, notes and papers, unless it is an open-book exam where the faculty specifies the material. Students bring their own basic calculator; scientific calculators are not allowed, and calculators cannot be borrowed or lent. Mobile phones must be switched off and left on the main table in the hall. A switched-off phone in a pocket is also not allowed. A phone found on a student is confiscated, with return fines of INR 2,000 the first time and INR 5,000 the second time.


### E.1.3 | Conduct during the examination
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** Exam Code of Conduct (4, 6, 8-10); Student Handbook §9
**Questions:** Can I go to the restroom during the exam? | Where do I do rough work? | Can I talk during the exam? | Are exams monitored by CCTV?
**Keywords:** restroom during exam, rough work, seat number, answer sheet, talking in exam, CCTV

Only one student at a time may visit the restroom, and only with the invigilator's permission. No talking, signalling, or exchange of question papers or materials is allowed, and looking at another student's answer book counts as an attempt to copy. Write the seat number and other particulars on every answer book and supplement, and get each sheet signed by the supervisor. Rough work is done only on the last page of the answer book, never on the question paper, and no extra sheets are given for it. Answers must be legible, and answer books must never leave the room. Exams are monitored on CCTV, and copying found later is acted on.


### E.1.4 | When students may leave the examination hall
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** Exam Code of Conduct (7, 10); Student Handbook §9 (13)
**Questions:** When can I leave the exam hall? | Can I submit early and leave? | Can I leave in the last few minutes?
**Keywords:** leave exam early, submit early, last minutes, finish early

AI & DS Code of Conduct: students cannot submit and leave before 30 minutes have passed, or during the last 5 minutes. Handbook rule: no leaving within the first half hour or the last 15 minutes, and early finishers may leave only after handing their answer book to the supervisor. In every case, students stay seated until all answer books are collected and must hand the answer book to the invigilator themselves. Where the two rules differ, following the stricter one (no leaving in the last 15 minutes) is safe.


## E.2 Unfair means


### E.2.1 | Unfair means (UFM) and cheating procedure
**Type:** procedure · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** Student Handbook §12.1; Exam Code of Conduct (5)
**Questions:** What happens if I am caught cheating? | What is unfair means (UFM)? | What is the penalty for copying in an exam?
**Keywords:** cheating, copying, chits, unfair means, UFM, caught in exam, malpractice

Copying, exchanging notes, carrying chits, talking to others, or using any electronic or internet device during an exam is unfair means (UFM). For a minor attempt, the invigilator may act on the spot: a warning, a seat change, or a mark deduction if the invigilator is the course faculty. For a serious attempt, the student is taken to another room, gives a written statement, and has the answer book written so far seized. The student then continues on a new answer book with no extra time. The invigilator's report, the student's statement and both answer books go to the Program Office. The Programme Chair refers the case to the Disciplinary Committee. Penalties can go up to expulsion, with a right to a mercy appeal to the Dean.


## E.3 Complete rule lists


### E.3.1 | Student Handbook examination rules (all 15)
**Type:** table · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** Student Handbook §9
**Questions:** What are all 15 Handbook exam rules? | Give me the full list of exam instructions
**Keywords:** handbook exam rules, full exam rules list, examination instructions

(1) Sit in the seat allotted on the seating chart, 10 minutes before the start. Late entry is at the exam coordinator's discretion, never after the first 30 minutes. (2) Wear the ID card at all times and produce it when asked, or you may not be allowed to sit the exam. (3) Do not leave the hall without the invigilator's permission. (4) Write legibly. (5) No extra supplements are given for rough work; use the last page of the main answer book. (6) Do not refer to any book, paper or notes unless it is an open-book exam. (7) In open-book exams, faculty suggest the permitted material. (8) Bring your own calculator, with no borrowing or lending. Books, papers, scientific calculators, mobiles, smart watches, iPads and Bluetooth devices are not allowed. (9) No verbal or non-verbal communication. (10) Passing, receiving or looking at others' answer papers is prohibited; a willing helper is as guilty as the receiver. (11) Get the main answer book and each sheet signed by the supervisor. (12) Exams are under CCTV, and copying found later is acted on. (13) Stay until answer books are collected. You may leave early only if you finish at least 15 minutes before the end, and never in the first half hour or the last 15 minutes. (14) Answer books must never leave the room, and handing them over is the student's responsibility. (15) Breaches can lead to penalties up to expulsion, decided through due process.


### E.3.2 | AI & DS Examination Code of Conduct (all 11 rules)
**Type:** table · **Applies to:** PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** PGDM (AI & DS) Examination Code of Conduct for Students
**Questions:** What is the AI & DS exam code of conduct? | List all 11 exam rules for AI & DS
**Keywords:** AI DS exam code of conduct, full list, exam instructions AI DS

(1) Bring the Institute ID card and hall ticket, or you will not be allowed in. (2) All electronic and Bluetooth devices, books, notes and other materials are prohibited. (3) Be at your place 15 minutes before the exam starts; no entry once it has begun. (4) Only one student at a time may go to the restroom, with the block invigilator's prior permission. (5) Copying, exchanging notes, carrying chits, talking, or using an electronic or internet device is unfair means (UFM), and severe penalties apply as per the Handbook. (6) No exchange of question papers or other materials. (7) No submitting and leaving before 30 minutes, and no leaving in the last 5 minutes. (8) Write your seat number and particulars on every answer book and supplement. (9) Rough work goes only on the last page of the answer book or supplement, never on the question paper. (10) Do not leave your seat until all answer books are collected. (11) Carry the I-Card during exam hours or pay a fine of INR 100; a lost hall ticket is replaced for a fine of INR 100.
