# Part F — Fees and finance

The fee structure and due dates, how to pay, what to do when a payment fails, late fees with worked examples, refunds, education loans, and every fine and charge.


### F.0 | Part F at a glance: fees, payments, late fees and refunds
**Type:** overview · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** OFFICIAL-DERIVED · **Source:** Consolidated from F.1.1 to F.2.4 (Admission Offer Annexure II, Late Fees Policy v02, Student Handbook; payment procedures are augmented)
**Questions:** Tell me everything about fees | Explain the complete fee structure and rules | What are all the fee details? | How do fees, late fees and refunds work?
**Keywords:** everything about fees, fee guide, complete fee rules, all fee details, fees explained, how fees work

**1. What you pay (official).** Total tuition for PGDM Batch 2025-27 is INR 10,00,000, plus a refundable caution deposit of INR 50,000, so INR 10,50,000 in all. It is paid in five parts: a registration fee of INR 2,00,000 on accepting the offer, then four installments of INR 2,00,000 due on 15 July 2025, 15 January 2026, 15 July 2026 and 15 January 2027. The caution deposit is paid with the 1st installment (so that payment is INR 2,50,000). The fee includes study material, books, library and internet; it excludes hostel, mess and personal expenses. BDA students are assumed to follow the same structure (to be confirmed with Accounts).

**2. Running totals (derived).** After registration INR 2,00,000; after the 1st installment INR 4,50,000; after the 2nd INR 6,50,000; after the 3rd INR 8,50,000; after the 4th INR 10,50,000. Year 1 payments total INR 6,50,000 and Year 2 payments INR 4,00,000.

**3. If you pay late (official, Late Fees Policy v02, effective 1 April 2025).** INR 100 per day for up to 2 weeks late, INR 200 per day from 2 to 4 weeks, INR 400 per day from 4 to 6 weeks. Payment can never be delayed beyond 6 weeks. After 6 weeks the student is barred from examinations and lectures; if the Institute allows attendance in an extraordinary case, the student pays the fee, the late fee and INR 2,000 per extra day, capped at 100% of the fee. Worked examples (progressive reading): 10 days late INR 1,000; 20 days INR 2,600; 35 days INR 7,000; 42 days INR 9,800; 45 days with permission INR 15,800. No request about late fees is entertained, so pay on time.

**4. Consequences of dues (official).** Grades, transcripts, the diploma and other services can be withheld. Unpaid late fees and fines are deducted from the caution deposit. A student with dues does not qualify for the PGDM award.

**5. How to pay (helpdesk guidance).** Online fee portal (net banking, UPI, cards) or NEFT/RTGS to the Institute account shared only through official Accounts channels. After NEFT/RTGS, email the UTR and enrollment number to Accounts. Receipts are issued within 2 working days. Education loans: Accounts issues a fee demand letter within 3 working days; apply to the bank at least 30 days before the due date because the due date does not move.

**6. Portal failure on the due date (helpdesk guidance).** Keep a screenshot and transaction ID, do not retry repeatedly if debited, and raise a "Fees & Payments" support request the same day. If the fault is verified on the Institute's portal and payment is completed by the next working day, no late fee is charged for that gap. Student-side failures do not qualify. A due date on a Sunday or holiday moves to the next working day.

**7. Refunds and withdrawal (helpdesk guidance modelled on AICTE norms).** Withdrawal before Trimester I begins: refund less up to INR 1,000 processing. After classes begin: the current installment is not refundable, future installments are not charged, and the caution deposit is refunded after dues. Refunds reach only the verified bank account within 30 days; changing that account needs identity verification and approval.

**8. Other charges and fines (official unless noted).** Mobile phone in class INR 500 spot fine; confiscated phone return INR 2,000 (first) / INR 5,000 (second); I-Card not carried in exams INR 100; replacement hall ticket INR 100; library overdue INR 5 per day per book; lost book: higher of latest-edition MRP or 3x the original price; ragging fine INR 25,000. Document charges (helpdesk): transcript INR 1,000, duplicate grade sheet INR 500, duplicate ID INR 500, duplicate diploma INR 2,000, re-assessment INR 5,000 per course, supplementary exam INR 2,500 per course.

**9. Who to contact.** Accounts: Mr. Ashok Rupera (Assistant Manager - Accounts), under Finance Controller Ms. Shruti Mehta. Program Office: 9099981586. For fee status, late-fee calculation or a receipt, use the student helpdesk after signing in.


## F.1 Fee structure


### F.1.1 | PGDM total tuition fee, inclusions and exclusions
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-AIDS (see FEE-005 for BDA) · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** Admission Offer Letter, Annexure II (Batch 2025-27)

**Keywords:** total fees, tuition fee, 10 lakh, what is included, hostel not included, mess

The total tuition fee for PGDM Batch 2025-27 is INR 10,00,000 (Ten Lakhs). It is paid as a registration fee plus four installments of INR 2,00,000 each. A refundable caution deposit of INR 50,000 is paid with the 1st installment. The programme fee includes study materials, books, library charges and internet charges. It does not include accommodation (hostel), mess charges or personal expenses.

Cumulative amount paid after each stage, including the caution deposit: registration INR 2,00,000; 1st installment (15 Jul 2025) INR 4,50,000; 2nd (15 Jan 2026) INR 6,50,000; 3rd (15 Jul 2026) INR 8,50,000; 4th (15 Jan 2027) INR 10,50,000. Tuition only: 2, 4, 6, 8 and 10 lakh. Payments due in the first academic year (Aug 2025 - Jul 2026, including registration): INR 6,50,000 including the INR 50,000 deposit. Payments due in the second year: INR 4,00,000 (3rd and 4th installments). If the 1st, 2nd and 3rd installments are paid, the remaining tuition is INR 2,00,000 (4th installment). **Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Project assumption (no BDA-specific fee annexure was provided)

For this knowledge base, PGDM-BDA Batch 2025-27 is assumed to have the same fee structure as PGDM-AI & DS: INR 10,00,000 tuition, a registration fee plus four installments of INR 2,00,000 due on the same dates (15 Jul 2025, 15 Jan 2026, 15 Jul 2026, 15 Jan 2027), and a refundable INR 50,000 caution deposit with the 1st installment. The Late Fees Policy applies to all students. BDA students should confirm their exact fee schedule with Accounts.

**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** Admission Offer Annexure II; Late Fees Policy v02 §3

A caution deposit of INR 50,000 is paid along with the 1st installment. It is refundable after the programme is completed, after any dues are adjusted. Unpaid late fees, penalties, library charges or damage recoveries are deducted from the caution deposit.


## F.2 Paying your fees


### F.2.1 | How to pay fees: modes, rules and safety
**Type:** procedure · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Helpdesk policy written for this project (not issued by AIDTM)
**Questions:** How do I pay my fees? | Can I pay by UPI or card? | Can I pay by NEFT or RTGS? | Is cash accepted? | Can I pay in parts? | Where do I get the bank account details?
**Keywords:** how to pay, payment mode, NEFT, RTGS, UPI, card, demand draft, cash, part payment, bank details

| Mode | How | When it counts as paid | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Online fee portal (UPI, net banking, debit or credit card) | Student portal, then Fees, then Pay | Instant acknowledgement; official receipt within 2 working days | Card convenience charges, if any, are paid by the student; UPI daily limits depend on your bank |
| NEFT or RTGS | To the Institute account shown on the fee portal or in an official Accounts email; quote your enrollment number | Same or next working day | Email the UTR number and your enrollment number to Accounts |
| Demand draft | In favour of "Adani Institute of Digital Technology Management" | On deposit at the Institute | Write your name and enrollment number on the back |
| Education loan disbursement | The bank pays the Institute against the demand letter (F.2.4) | When the bank's payment is received | Must reach the Institute by the due date |
| Cash | Not accepted | — | — |

**Rules:**

- Pay the full installment, net of any scholarship adjustment (G.2.1).
- Part payment is accepted only with Accounts' prior approval. The late fee then runs on the unpaid balance.
- The date the money reaches the Institute is the payment date.
- Keep every receipt until you receive your diploma.

**Safety:**

- Take bank details only from the fee portal or an official @aidtm.ac.in email, and treat any message with "new bank details" as suspected fraud.
- Never share card numbers, CVVs, PINs or OTPs with anyone, including staff or the helpdesk (N.1.3).


### F.2.2 | Payment portal failure or technical problem on the due date
**Type:** procedure · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Helpdesk policy written for this project (not issued by AIDTM)
**Questions:** The payment portal failed, what should I do? | Can I pay on the next working day if the portal is down? | Money was debited but the fee is not showing | Will I be charged a late fee if the payment failed? | What if the due date is a Sunday?
**Keywords:** payment failed, portal down, transaction failed, next working day, amount debited, late fee waiver, gateway

**Step by step:**

1. Check whether money left your account (bank SMS or statement).
2. If nothing was debited, try once more later, or pay by NEFT/RTGS the same day (F.2.1).
3. If money was debited but you have no receipt, do not pay again. Wait 2 hours and note the transaction ID or UTR.
4. Raise a "Fees & Payments" support request with priority High. Attach a screenshot and give the time, the amount and the transaction ID.
5. Accounts checks with the payment gateway within 3 working days:
   - If the money reached the Institute, the receipt is issued.
   - If the payment failed, your bank reverses the debit, usually within 5-7 working days, and you pay again.

**Late-fee protection:**

| Situation | Late fee? |
|---|---|
| Fault verified on the Institute's portal or gateway, and payment completed by the next working day (or within 1 working day of Accounts' confirmation) | No, treated as on time |
| Failure on the student's side (insufficient balance, daily limit, wrong UPI ID, expired card) | Yes, the normal slabs apply (F.3.1) |
| Due date falls on a Sunday or Institute holiday | No, the next working day is treated as the due date |

The official Late Fees Policy entertains no requests regarding late fees (F.3.4). The portal-failure protection above is a helpdesk rule for verified Institute-side faults only.

**Contacts:** Accounts (Mr. Ashok Rupera, Assistant Manager - Accounts); Program Office 9099981586.


### F.2.3 | Fee receipts and checking your fee status
**Type:** procedure · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Helpdesk policy written for this project (not issued by AIDTM)
**Questions:** How do I get my fee receipt? | I paid but did not get a receipt | How can I check my fee status? | How do I get a duplicate receipt?
**Keywords:** fee receipt, payment proof, receipt not received, fee status, duplicate receipt

- **Online portal payment:** an acknowledgement appears at once, and the official receipt is emailed to your Institute address within 2 working days.
- **NEFT/RTGS:** the payment shows on your fee record after Accounts matches your UTR, usually within 2 working days of your email.
- **Checking status:** sign in to the student helpdesk and ask "What is my fee status?" to see every installment (paid, pending, overdue), any late fee accrued, the amount outstanding and the next due date. The same is on the student portal.
- **Duplicate receipt:** free, within 2 working days, through a "Fees & Payments" support request.
- **Missing payment:** if a payment is not reflected after 3 working days, raise a High-priority request with the UTR.

Only you can see your own fee record (N.1.1).


### F.2.4 | Education loans
**Type:** procedure · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Helpdesk policy written for this project (not issued by AIDTM); no-waiver rule from Late Fees Policy v02 (official)
**Questions:** How do I get an education loan? | What documents does the bank need from AIDTM? | Will the late fee apply if my loan is delayed? | Does the bank pay the Institute directly? | Is education loan interest tax deductible?
**Keywords:** education loan, bank loan, loan documents, fee demand letter, disbursement, 80E

**Documents the Institute provides (within 3 working days of a "Certificates & Documents" or "Fees & Payments" request):**

- Fee demand letter on letterhead, with the installment-wise schedule and the Institute's bank details.
- Bonafide certificate.
- Copy of the admission letter and fee structure (F.1.2).
- Reference to the AICTE approval, which is published on the AIDTM website (A.1.6).

**Timeline:**

- Apply to the bank at least 30 days before the installment due date.
- For tranche-wise loans, ask for a fresh demand letter before each installment.

**Who pays:** the bank pays the Institute directly, and Accounts sends the receipt to the student.

**Responsibility for the due date:** the due date does not move because a loan is delayed. Late fees apply from the day after the due date, and the policy entertains no waiver requests (F.3.4). If your loan is delayed, pay the installment yourself and claim a reimbursement from the bank.

**Tax:** interest on an education loan may be deductible under Section 80E of the Income Tax Act. Check with a tax adviser.


## F.3 Late fees and consequences of dues


### F.3.1 | Late fee for tuition: daily slabs
**Type:** table · **Applies to:** All AIDTM students · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** AIDTM Late Fees Policy (AP-LFP), version 02, effective 1 April 2025
**Questions:** What is the late fee? | How much is the late fee per day? | What happens if I pay late? | What are the late fee slabs?
**Keywords:** late fee, late payment, penalty per day, missed due date, 100 per day, 200, 400

A late fee is charged when tuition is not paid by the notified last date. Up to 2 weeks late: INR 100 per day. From 2 to 4 weeks late: INR 200 per day. From 4 to 6 weeks late: INR 400 per day. Payment can in no case be delayed beyond 6 weeks. The policy aims to ensure timely payment and avoid disciplinary action. It states that no request regarding late fees will be entertained, so parents and students are asked to pay on time.


### F.3.2 | How the late fee is calculated: worked examples
**Type:** table · **Applies to:** All AIDTM students · **Status:** OFFICIAL-DERIVED · **Source:** Derived from Late Fees Policy v02 §1
**Questions:** How is the late fee calculated? | How much late fee for 10 days? | How much late fee for 20 days late? | What is the maximum late fee in 6 weeks?
**Keywords:** calculate late fee, how much late fee, example, 10 days late, 20 days late, total penalty

Interpretation used: each day is charged at the rate of the band it falls in. Days 1-14 are charged at INR 100 per day, days 15-28 at INR 200 per day, and days 29-42 at INR 400 per day. Examples: 10 days late = 10 x 100 = INR 1,000. 20 days late = 1,400 + (6 x 200) = INR 2,600. 35 days late = 1,400 + 2,800 + (7 x 400) = INR 7,000. 42 days late (the 6-week maximum) = 1,400 + 2,800 + 5,600 = INR 9,800. If attendance is permitted beyond 6 weeks, INR 2,000 is added per extra day; for example, 45 days late = 9,800 + 6,000 = INR 15,800. The total penalty is capped at 100% of the fees. Accounts makes the final computation, and an alternative reading of the policy charges the whole period at the band rate reached.


### F.3.3 | Non-payment beyond 6 weeks
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** All AIDTM students · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** Late Fees Policy v02 §1
**Questions:** What happens if I don't pay for more than 6 weeks? | Can I sit exams if my fees are unpaid? | What is the 2000 rupees per day penalty?
**Keywords:** not paid 6 weeks, debarred from exam, cannot attend class, 2000 per day, maximum penalty

If fees remain unpaid more than 6 weeks after the notified last date, the student is not allowed to appear in examinations or attend lectures. In an extraordinary situation the Institute may permit such a student to attend class or take the examination. The student must then pay the fees plus the late fee plus an additional penalty of INR 2,000 per day beyond 6 weeks. The maximum penalty is limited to 100% of the fees.


### F.3.4 | Requests to waive late fees
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** All AIDTM students · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** Late Fees Policy v02 §4; Admission Offer Annexure II
**Questions:** Can the late fee be waived? | Can I get an extension for my fees? | Who can change the fee due dates?
**Keywords:** waive late fee, extension request, can I get more time, exemption

Under the official Late Fees Policy, no request regarding late fees will be entertained, and students and parents are asked to pay on time to avoid inconvenience. Any change to payment dates is decided only by the AIDTM Admission Committee and published on the Institute website. The only waiver this knowledge base describes is the verified Institute-portal failure case in F.2.2, which is AUGMENTED.


### F.3.5 | Consequences of outstanding dues
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** All AIDTM students · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** Late Fees Policy v02 §1, §3; Student Handbook §11
**Questions:** What happens if I have outstanding dues? | Will my transcript be withheld for unpaid fees? | Are unpaid late fees deducted from the caution deposit?
**Keywords:** dues pending, transcript withheld, grades withheld, degree withheld, outstanding

The Institute may withhold grades, transcripts, degrees/diplomas and other services from students who have not met their financial obligations in full. Late fee and penalty charges must be cleared promptly when due; if they are not paid, they are adjusted from the caution deposit. A student with outstanding dues does not qualify for the award of the PGDM.


## F.4 Refunds and other charges


### F.4.1 | Withdrawal from the programme and refunds
**Type:** procedure · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Helpdesk policy written for this project (not issued by AIDTM), modelled on AICTE refund norms (the official AIDTM refund policy was not provided)
**Questions:** How do I withdraw from the programme? | Will I get a refund if I leave? | When is the caution deposit refunded? | How long does a refund take? | Where is the refund paid?
**Keywords:** refund, withdraw admission, cancel admission, leave programme, money back, caution deposit refund, no dues

| When you withdraw | Tuition | Caution deposit | Processing charge |
|---|---|---|---|
| Before Trimester I begins | All fees paid are refunded | Refunded | Up to INR 1,000 |
| After classes begin | The current installment period is not refundable; future installments are not charged | Refunded after dues are adjusted | — |
| Seat cancelled by the Institute (misconduct, or failing the award criteria) | No refund for the current period | Refunded after dues are adjusted | — |
| On completing the programme | — | Refunded after no-dues clearance, within 30 days | — |

**Process:**

1. Write to the Program Office with your reason for withdrawing.
2. Complete the no-dues form, signed by the Library, Accounts, IT and (for residents) the Hostel.
3. Accounts processes the refund within 30 days of approval, only to your verified bank account.

Changing the refund bank account is a sensitive change that needs verification and approval (N.1.2). The hostel deposit is refunded after the room inspection (L.1.2).

This table is modelled on the AICTE fee-refund norms, under which an institute may keep a processing fee of up to INR 1,000 when a student withdraws before the programme starts. Where this summary differs, AICTE regulations prevail.


### F.4.2 | Summary of fines and charges across policies
**Type:** table · **Applies to:** All AIDTM students · **Status:** OFFICIAL-DERIVED · **Source:** Consolidated from Late Fees Policy v02, Exam Code of Conduct, Student Handbook §12.3 and Annexures VII, VIII, IX
**Questions:** What are all the fines? | How much is the fine for using a phone in class? | What is the fine for losing my hall ticket? | What is the library fine?
**Keywords:** fines list, penalty amount, how much fine, mobile fine, ID card fine, hall ticket fine, library fine, ragging fine

| Situation | Amount (INR) |
|---|---|
| Tuition late fee | 100 / 200 / 400 per day (up to 2, 2-4, 4-6 weeks) |
| Tuition unpaid beyond 6 weeks, if permitted to attend | +2,000 per day, capped at 100% of the fees |
| Mobile phone used in or around class | 500 (spot fine) |
| Confiscated phone returned (1st / 2nd time) | 2,000 / 5,000; 3rd time the phone is confiscated permanently and referred to the Disciplinary Committee |
| Not carrying I-Card during exams | 100 |
| Replacement hall ticket | 100 |
| Overdue library book | 5 per day per book (up to 60 days) |
| Ragging | Fine of 25,000, among other punishments |
| Lost or damaged library book | Higher of the latest edition's MRP or 3x the original price |

# Part G — Scholarships and financial assistance

The four Institute scholarships, who qualifies, how much they are worth, how to apply, what each status means, how to keep a scholarship, and external schemes.


### G.0 | Part G at a glance: all scholarships
**Type:** overview · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Consolidated from G.1.1 to G.2.3 (sample scholarship policy for this project; ragging withdrawal rule is official)
**Questions:** Tell me everything about scholarships | Which scholarships can I get? | Explain all scholarship rules
**Keywords:** everything about scholarships, scholarship guide, all scholarships, scholarship rules explained, which scholarship can I get

**1. The four scholarships.** (a) Merit Scholarship at Admission: automatic, based on entrance percentile and graduation marks; Tier 1 (95th percentile or above) INR 1,00,000, Tier 2 (85th to below 95th) INR 50,000, split equally across the four installments. (b) Academic Excellence Scholarship (Year 2): automatic for the top three students per programme by Year 1 CGPA after Trimester III results; INR 75,000 / 50,000 / 25,000, adjusted against the 3rd and 4th installments; needs no F or I grade, no disciplinary action and 70% attendance in every course. (c) Need-Based Financial Assistance: application-based for family income below INR 6,00,000 a year; up to INR 1,00,000 per year; documents are an income certificate or parents' ITR, a declaration and the latest grade sheet; closing dates 15 June (July installment) and 15 December (January installment). (d) Women in Data & AI Scholarship: up to five women per batch, INR 50,000 each, based on Trimester I GPA and a statement of purpose; closes four weeks after Trimester I results; adjusted against the next two installments.

**2. Common rules.** Scholarships are fee concessions adjusted against tuition installments, never cash. Only one scholarship can be held at a time; if eligible for more than one, the higher amount applies. They never cover the caution deposit, late fees or fines. Late fees, if any, are calculated on the net amount after adjustment.

**3. How to apply and timelines.** Raise a "Scholarships" support request or submit the form to the Program Office with the documents. The Scholarship Committee (Dean, Programme Chair, Finance Controller) decides within 21 working days of the closing date and emails the decision. Approved amounts are adjusted against the next due installment.

**4. Status meanings.** Applied (received); Under Review (being evaluated); Approved (granted, amounts confirmed); Adjusted (applied to the fee record); On Hold (document or condition missing, student informed); Rejected (reason communicated); Withdrawn (cancelled after a breach or at the student's request). Withdrawing an application is a sensitive change that needs approval.

**5. Keeping the scholarship.** Maintain CGPA of at least 2.60, no F or unresolved I grade, meet attendance rules, and have no misconduct finding. On a breach, the remaining unadjusted amount is withdrawn from the next installment; amounts already adjusted are recovered only for misconduct or false documents. Under the official anti-ragging rules, withdrawing a scholarship is one punishment for ragging.

**6. Privacy.** A student can see only their own scholarship status, after signing in.


## G.1 Scholarship schemes


### G.1.1 | Scholarship schemes at a glance and common rules
**Type:** table · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Sample scholarship policy written for this project (AIDTM scholarship rules were not provided)
**Questions:** What scholarships does AIDTM offer? | How much are the scholarships worth? | Can I get two scholarships? | Are scholarships paid in cash? | Does the scholarship cover the caution deposit or late fees?
**Keywords:** scholarship types, fee concession, scholarship amount, two scholarships, scholarship rules

| Scheme | Who it is for | Amount (INR) | Application needed? | Adjusted against | Deadline |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Merit Scholarship at Admission (G.1.2) | Entrance-test toppers | Tier 1: 1,00,000; Tier 2: 50,000 | No, automatic | 1st to 4th installments, equally | At admission |
| Academic Excellence Scholarship (G.1.3) | Top 3 per programme by Year 1 CGPA | 75,000 / 50,000 / 25,000 | No, automatic | 3rd and 4th installments | After Trim III results |
| Need-Based Financial Assistance (G.1.4) | Family income below 6,00,000 a year | Up to 1,00,000 a year | Yes | Next due installment | 15 June and 15 December |
| Women in Data & AI Scholarship (G.1.5) | Women students, up to 5 per batch | 50,000 | Yes | Next two installments | 4 weeks after Trim I results |

**Rules common to every scheme:**

- **Not cash.** A scholarship is a fee concession adjusted against tuition installments. You pay only the net amount.
- **One at a time.** If you qualify for more than one scheme, you receive the higher amount.
- **What it never covers:** the caution deposit, late fees, fines, hostel, mess or transport charges.
- **Late fees:** calculated on the net amount due after the adjustment.
- **Who decides:** the Scholarship Committee (the Dean, the Programme Chair and the Finance Controller).
- **Appeals:** to the Dean within 7 days of the decision. The Dean's decision is final.
- **Privacy:** you can see only your own scholarship status, after signing in (N.1.1).


### G.1.2 | Merit Scholarship at Admission
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Sample scholarship policy written for this project (AIDTM scholarship rules were not provided)
**Questions:** What is the merit scholarship? | What percentile do I need for a scholarship? | How much is the Tier 1 scholarship? | Do I need to apply for the merit scholarship? | How is the merit scholarship paid?
**Keywords:** merit scholarship, CAT percentile, entrance score, admission scholarship, tier 1, tier 2

**Eligibility:** awarded automatically at admission, based on the entrance-test score. Graduation marks of at least 60% are also required.

| Tier | CAT / XAT / MAT / CMAT / ATMA percentile | GMAT score | Amount (INR) | Adjusted per installment (INR) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Tier 1 | 95 and above | 650 and above | 1,00,000 | 25,000 against each of the 1st to 4th installments |
| Tier 2 | 85 to below 95 | 600 to 649 | 50,000 | 12,500 against each of the 1st to 4th installments |

**How you are told:** the award is stated in the admission offer letter, and the adjustment appears on your fee record. No application is needed.

**Keeping it:** the remaining installments' adjustments continue only while you meet the continuation conditions in G.2.3 (CGPA of at least 2.60, no F or I grade, and attendance and conduct in order).


### G.1.3 | Academic Excellence Scholarship (Year 2)
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Sample scholarship policy written for this project (AIDTM scholarship rules were not provided)
**Questions:** What is the academic excellence scholarship? | How do I qualify for the topper scholarship? | How much do the top 3 students get? | When is the academic excellence scholarship announced?
**Keywords:** academic excellence, CGPA scholarship, topper, rank scholarship, second year scholarship

**Who:** the top three students in each PGDM programme (BDA and AI & DS ranked separately) by Year 1 CGPA, calculated after Trimester III results.

| Rank | Amount (INR) | Adjustment |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 75,000 | Half against the 3rd installment, half against the 4th |
| 2 | 50,000 | Half against the 3rd installment, half against the 4th |
| 3 | 25,000 | Half against the 3rd installment, half against the 4th |

**Eligibility conditions:** no F or I grade in Year 1, no disciplinary finding, and at least 70% attendance in every course.

**Tie-break:** higher Trimester III GPA first, then higher overall attendance.

**Timing:** announced by the Program Office by 31 August, after the SIP period. If the 3rd installment has already been paid, the full amount is adjusted against the 4th installment.

No application is needed.


### G.1.4 | Need-Based Financial Assistance
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Sample scholarship policy written for this project (AIDTM scholarship rules were not provided)
**Questions:** Is there financial help for students from low-income families? | What is the income limit for need-based scholarship? | What documents do I need for need-based assistance? | When is the need-based scholarship deadline?
**Keywords:** need based, financial hardship, family income, low income, fee support, income certificate

**Who:** students whose total annual family income is below INR 6,00,000.

| Annual family income (INR) | Assistance per year (INR, up to) |
|---|---|
| Below 3,00,000 | 1,00,000 |
| 3,00,000 to below 4,50,000 | 75,000 |
| 4,50,000 to below 6,00,000 | 50,000 |

**Documents:**

- Income certificate from the competent authority, or the parents'/guardians' ITR for the last financial year.
- Student's self-declaration of family income and dependants.
- Latest grade sheet (after Trimester I).
- Bank statement of the main earning member for 6 months, if asked.

**Process:**

- Applications close on 15 June (for the July installment) and 15 December (for the January installment).
- The Scholarship Committee may call the student for a short interview, and verifies the documents.
- The decision comes within 21 working days of the closing date.
- The award is renewed each year on a fresh application.

**False documents:** lead to cancellation, recovery of the amounts already adjusted, and disciplinary action.


### G.1.5 | Women in Data & AI Scholarship
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Sample scholarship policy written for this project (AIDTM scholarship rules were not provided)
**Questions:** Is there a scholarship for women students? | How do I apply for the Women in Data & AI scholarship? | How many women scholarships are there? | How is the women scholarship selected?
**Keywords:** women scholarship, girls scholarship, female students, gender scholarship

**Who:** women students in either PGDM programme. Up to five awards per batch.

**Amount:** INR 50,000, adjusted as INR 25,000 against each of the next two installments.

**Selection:**

- 60% weight: Trimester I GPA.
- 40% weight: a statement of purpose of up to 500 words on the student's goals in data and AI.
- Shortlisted candidates may be interviewed.

**How to apply:** submit the statement through a "Scholarships" support request within 4 weeks of Trimester I results being declared.

**Continuation conditions:** as in G.2.3.

**Combining with other awards:** the scheme cannot be combined with another Institute scholarship. If you qualify for both, the higher amount applies.


## G.2 Applying, status and keeping your scholarship


### G.2.1 | How to apply for a scholarship and how the money is applied
**Type:** procedure · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Sample scholarship policy written for this project (AIDTM scholarship rules were not provided)
**Questions:** How do I apply for a scholarship? | How long does a scholarship decision take? | How is the scholarship adjusted in my fees? | Where do I submit the scholarship form?
**Keywords:** apply scholarship, scholarship form, documents, decision time, how long, adjustment

1. **Check eligibility** in G.1.1. Merit and Academic Excellence awards are automatic; Need-Based and Women in Data & AI need an application.
2. **Apply:** raise a "Scholarships" support request, or hand the scholarship form to the Program Office, with every document listed for that scheme. Incomplete applications are put On Hold.
3. **Review:** the Scholarship Committee (Dean, Programme Chair, Finance Controller) evaluates the application and may hold a short interview.
4. **Decision:** within 21 working days of the closing date, sent to your Institute email. The status becomes Approved or Rejected.
5. **Adjustment:** Accounts applies the amount to your next due installment, and the status becomes Adjusted. Your fee record then shows the net amount to pay.
6. **Appeal:** if rejected, you may appeal to the Dean within 7 days.

Track the status at any time by asking the helpdesk "What is my scholarship status?" (G.2.2).


### G.2.2 | Scholarship status: what each stage means
**Type:** table · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Sample scholarship policy written for this project (AIDTM scholarship rules were not provided)
**Questions:** What does my scholarship status mean? | What does Under Review mean? | What does Adjusted mean? | Why is my scholarship on hold?
**Keywords:** scholarship status, under review, approved, rejected, on hold, adjusted, withdrawn

| Status | Meaning | What you should do |
|---|---|---|
| Applied | Application received | Nothing; wait for review |
| Under Review | The Committee is evaluating it (decision within 21 working days) | Respond quickly if asked for an interview or a document |
| On Hold | A document is missing or a condition is unmet | Upload what is asked through a "Scholarships" request |
| Approved | Granted; the amount and installments are confirmed | Check the net amount on your fee record |
| Adjusted | The amount has been applied to your fee record | Pay the net balance by the due date |
| Rejected | Not granted; the reason is emailed | You may appeal to the Dean within 7 days |
| Withdrawn | Cancelled after a breach of conditions, or at your request after approval | See G.2.3 |
| None | No scholarship held | See G.1.1 for schemes you can apply for |

Withdrawing your own application is a sensitive change that needs approval (N.1.2).

**Status names in the helpdesk system.** The helpdesk records a scholarship in one of these states: NOT_APPLIED, SUBMITTED (= Applied), UNDER_REVIEW, APPROVED (an approved scholarship that is being adjusted against fees is still APPROVED; the adjusted and remaining amounts are shown separately), ADDITIONAL_DOCUMENTS_REQUIRED (= On Hold), REJECTED and WITHDRAWN. The helpdesk reports your recorded status; it does not decide eligibility.

### G.2.3 | Keeping your scholarship: continuation conditions
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Sample scholarship policy written for this project (AIDTM scholarship rules were not provided); ragging punishment from Student Handbook Annexure VIII (official)
**Questions:** How can I lose my scholarship? | What CGPA do I need to keep my scholarship? | What happens to my scholarship if I get an F? | Will I have to return the scholarship money?
**Keywords:** lose scholarship, scholarship cancelled, continuation criteria, CGPA requirement, recover scholarship

| Condition | Requirement | If breached |
|---|---|---|
| Academic performance | CGPA of at least 2.60 at each trimester-end | Remaining unadjusted amount withdrawn from the next installment |
| Grades | No F grade, and no I grade left uncleared beyond its deadline | Same as above |
| Attendance | At least 70% in every course | Same as above |
| Conduct | No finding of academic or non-academic misconduct | Withdrawn; recovery of amounts already adjusted is possible |
| Ragging | Zero tolerance | Withdrawal of scholarship is one of the official punishments for ragging (J.3.1) |
| Honest documents | All documents genuine | Cancelled, amounts recovered, disciplinary action |

**Amounts already adjusted** are not recovered for academic shortfalls, only for misconduct or false documents.

**Restoration:** a student whose CGPA recovers to 2.60 or above can ask the Committee to restore the scholarship for the following installments.


### G.2.4 | Government and external scholarships
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Helpdesk policy written for this project (not issued by AIDTM)
**Questions:** Are there government scholarships for PGDM students? | Can I apply for external scholarships? | Can I hold an external scholarship and an AIDTM scholarship?
**Keywords:** government scholarship, national scholarship portal, external scholarship, state scholarship, CSR scholarship

**Where to look:**

- **Central schemes:** listed on the National Scholarship Portal (scholarships.gov.in).
- **State schemes:** run by state governments; check your home state's portal.
- **Other sources:** many banks, corporates (CSR scholarships) and foundations run merit and need-based schemes for postgraduate management and technology students.

**What the Institute provides:** bonafide certificates, fee structure letters and verification of enrollment (D.4.2), usually within 3 working days.

**Declaration:** you must tell the Program Office about any external scholarship you receive.

**Combining with an Institute scholarship:**

- An external award that covers the full tuition replaces the Institute award.
- A partial external award can usually be held together with an Institute scholarship, as long as the combined amount does not exceed the tuition due.

# Part I — Summer Internship, Capstone and Placements

The SIP and Industrial Capstone policy, how they are evaluated, paperwork and stipends, the placement process and its rules, the placement season, and the Institute's track record.


### I.0 | Part I at a glance: SIP, capstone and placements
**Type:** overview · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** OFFICIAL-DERIVED · **Source:** Consolidated from I.1.1 to I.3.7 (SIP & Placement Policy 2025-27 official; process steps and evaluation schemes augmented)
**Questions:** Tell me everything about placements | Explain the internship and placement rules | How do SIP, capstone and placements work?
**Keywords:** everything about placements, internship guide, placement rules explained, SIP and capstone guide, placement process

**Mandatory and no guarantee (official).** SIP and the Industrial Capstone are compulsory for the diploma; their purpose is learning; neither guarantees a PPO or a job. SIP is typically 5 months and the capstone typically 4 months, between Year 1 and Year 2, anywhere in India.

**Opportunities (official).** At most five facilitated interview opportunities per student across SIP and capstone/placements; shortlisting is the recruiter's decision; after five, the Institute owes no more. Self-sourced opportunities need prior approval and written notice.

**Offers (official).** Accept within the stated deadline or it counts as rejection. Once accepted you must join; not joining or leaving midway disqualifies you from all further processes. A PPO must be accepted; declining bars you from the whole drive. After being placed you can sit only for roles paying at least 1.5x your CTC (INR 6 LPA allows only above INR 9 LPA), and accepting the higher offer withdraws the earlier one.

**Opting out and special cases (official).** Opt out by email to the Program Office and Placement Office; it is final. Adani Group employees do not join placement drives; the Institute can arrange a non-Adani SIP but does not facilitate their capstone, placement or internal job postings.

**Conduct (official).** Recruiters can terminate for misbehaviour, non-cooperation or non-performance, which counts as a violation and can affect graduation and placement eligibility. Formal dress with a blazer throughout the placement process.

**Process and eligibility (helpdesk guidance).** Sign the policy declaration; upload a CV in the Institute format for approval; attend pre-placement talks; apply within deadlines; tests and interviews count toward the five opportunities; eligibility CGPA at least 2.60, no pending F or I, 70% attendance, no disciplinary finding, no dues (exceptions with Programme Chair approval). SIP grading: mentor evaluation 30%, report 40%, presentation and viva 30%. Capstone grading: proposal 10%, mid-review 20%, mentor 20%, report 30%, presentation and viva 20%; similarity index below 15%.

**Track record (website).** 100% placements, highest 20 LPA, lowest 8 LPA, 90% PPOs; recruiters include Accenture, Adani, Deloitte, EY, IBM, Infosys, KPMG, PwC, Quantiphi, TCS, Tech Mahindra and Tiger Analytics.


## I.1 Summer Internship and Industrial Capstone


### I.1.1 | SIP and Industrial Capstone: purpose and mandatory status
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** SIP, Industrial Capstone Project & Placement Policy 2025-27 §1-2; Student Handbook §5
**Questions:** Is the summer internship compulsory? | Does SIP guarantee a PPO or a job? | Who arranges internships?
**Keywords:** summer internship, SIP mandatory, capstone mandatory, PPO guarantee, internship purpose

The Summer Internship Program (SIP) and the Industrial Capstone Project are mandatory academic requirements for the PGDM diploma. Their main purpose is learning and industry exposure. Taking part does not guarantee a Pre-Placement Offer (PPO) or a final job, which depend on individual performance and company policies. The Placement Cell manages the process of finding internships. The policy applies to all BDA and AI & DS students of Batch 2025-27 and is binding once the student signs the consent declaration.


### I.1.2 | SIP and Capstone duration, timing and location
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** SIP Policy 2025-27 §3
**Questions:** How long is the summer internship? | How long is the capstone project? | When is the SIP? | Where will my internship be?
**Keywords:** internship duration, how many months, capstone duration, internship location, PAN India

The SIP is typically 5 months and the Industrial Capstone Project typically 4 months; durations may vary with the organisation's requirements. The SIP and Capstone are scheduled between the first and second years of the programme. Locations can be anywhere in India. The older Handbook figure of a 4-month SIP is superseded by this policy.


### I.1.3 | SIP and capstone approach (website)
**Type:** profile · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** AIDTM website (Placement)
**Questions:** Why does the SIP matter for placements? | What are the steps of the capstone project?
**Keywords:** why internship matters, SIP report, capstone steps, capstone framework

AIDTM describes summer internships as "practical avenues of knowledge and learning" with top organisations. Final-placement interviews focus mainly on the student's internship report. Capstone projects are interdisciplinary and require skills from several subject areas. They follow four steps: choose a topic, do a literature or documentation review, formulate a thesis, and present the research. The capstone shows the student's proficiency and what they have learned over the programme.


### I.1.4 | Conduct during SIP and Capstone
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** SIP Policy 2025-27 §6; Student Handbook §12.4
**Questions:** Can a company terminate my internship? | What happens if I am terminated from my internship?
**Keywords:** internship termination, behaviour during internship, fired from internship

Recruiters can end an internship or placement for inappropriate behaviour, non-cooperation or continued non-performance. Such a termination is a policy violation and may affect academic completion and placement eligibility. Unethical practices during SIP or placements are also dealt with severely under the Handbook.


## I.2 Evaluation, paperwork and stipend


### I.2.1 | Summer Internship evaluation
**Type:** procedure · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Sample evaluation scheme built for this project; SIP credits and mandatory status are official
**Questions:** How is the summer internship graded? | How long should the SIP report be? | When is the SIP report due? | When are SIP presentations?
**Keywords:** SIP evaluation, internship report, internship grade, internship presentation, company feedback

The Summer Internship Program (6 credits) is graded on: the company mentor's evaluation (30%), the final internship report of 40-60 pages following the Institute template with APA referencing (40%), and a presentation and viva before a faculty panel (30%). Students submit a fortnightly progress log to their faculty mentor. The report is due within two weeks of the internship ending, and presentations are held in the first month of Trimester IV. Plagiarism checks apply to the report. An I grade is given if the report or presentation is pending, and it becomes an F if not cleared in the following trimester.


### I.2.2 | Capstone Project evaluation
**Type:** procedure · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Sample evaluation scheme built for this project; the capstone's mandatory status is official
**Questions:** How is the capstone evaluated? | What is the capstone marks breakdown? | What similarity percentage is allowed in the capstone report?
**Keywords:** capstone evaluation, capstone report, capstone viva, capstone marks, capstone mentor

The Industrial Capstone Project (12 credits) is graded on: the approved proposal (10%), the mid-term review (20%), the company or client mentor's evaluation (20%), the final report (30%) and the final presentation and viva (20%). The Capstone Seminar & Viva course (3 credits) is graded on the weekly seminar participation and the viva. Each student has a faculty mentor and a host-organisation mentor. The final report must pass the plagiarism check with a similarity index below 15%. Satisfactory completion of the capstone is required for the award of the PGDM (official).


### I.2.3 | SIP paperwork, NOC, stipend and confidentiality
**Type:** procedure · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Helpdesk policy written for this project (not issued by AIDTM); self-sourcing approval, location and termination rules from SIP Policy 2025-27 (official)
**Questions:** Do I get a stipend during the internship? | How do I get an NOC for my internship? | Can I find my own internship? | Who pays for travel and accommodation during SIP? | Can I use company data in my SIP report?
**Keywords:** internship stipend, NOC, internship paperwork, self sourced internship, NDA, internship travel, accommodation

**Before the SIP:**

- Share the offer letter with the Placement Office.
- Get the NOC (issued within 3 working days, D.4.2).
- Sign the SIP undertaking.
- Note your assigned faculty mentor.
- Self-sourced internships need prior approval and formal written notice to the Institute (official, SIP Policy §4).

**During the SIP:**

- Submit a fortnightly progress log to your faculty mentor and attend the mid-SIP review call.
- Follow the company's working hours and leave rules.
- Institute conduct rules still apply. Termination by the company for misbehaviour, non-cooperation or non-performance is a policy violation (official, I.1.4).

**Stipend:**

- Decided and paid directly by the company. It is not guaranteed, and the amount varies by organisation.
- Unpaid internships are allowed when the Placement Office approves their learning value.
- A stipend does not change your fees.

**Travel and stay:** internships can be anywhere in India (official). Travel and accommodation are at the student's cost unless the company provides them.

**Confidentiality:**

- Follow the company's NDA.
- Use only masked or approved data in your report and presentation.
- Never upload company data to public AI tools (D.5.1).

**After the SIP:**

- Get the completion certificate and the company mentor's evaluation form.
- Submit the report within 2 weeks of finishing and present it in the first month of Trimester IV (I.2.1).


## I.3 Placement rules and process


### I.3.1 | Interview opportunities and self-sourced internships
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** SIP Policy 2025-27 §4
**Questions:** How many interview opportunities do I get? | What if I don't get placed after 5 interviews? | Can I find my own job or internship?
**Keywords:** how many interviews, five interviews, interview chances, find my own internship, off campus

The Institute facilitates at most five interview opportunities per student across SIP and Industrial Capstone / final placements, subject to eligibility. Shortlisting and selection are entirely the recruiter's decision. If a student has not secured a place after five interviews, the Institute is not obliged to provide more. Students may find their own SIP, Capstone or placement, but only with prior approval and formal written notice to the Institute.


### I.3.2 | Accepting offers, joining obligations and PPOs
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** SIP Policy 2025-27 §5
**Questions:** How long do I have to accept an offer? | Can I reject an offer after accepting it? | Is it compulsory to accept a PPO? | What happens if I don't join after accepting?
**Keywords:** accept offer, reject offer, deadline to accept, not joining, PPO mandatory, renege

Offer acceptance must be communicated within the timeline set by the company and the Placement Office; silence counts as rejection. Once an offer is accepted, the student must join. Not joining, or leaving an internship midway after it starts, is a violation and disqualifies the student from all further processes. A PPO received during the SIP must be accepted, and declining it bars the student from the entire placement drive.


### I.3.3 | Rules after being placed: the 1.5x rule
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** SIP Policy 2025-27 §7
**Questions:** Can I sit for placements after I am placed? | What is the 1.5 times rule? | What is a dream offer?
**Keywords:** already placed, second placement, dream offer, upgrade offer, 1.5 times CTC

Once placed, a student cannot take part in further placement drives unless the new role pays at least 1.5 times the annual CTC of the existing offer. For example, a student holding INR 6 LPA can apply only for roles paying more than INR 9 LPA. Accepting a higher offer automatically withdraws the student from the earlier accepted offer.


### I.3.4 | Opting out of placements
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** SIP Policy 2025-27 §8
**Questions:** How do I opt out of placements? | Can I come back to placements after opting out?
**Keywords:** opt out of placement, higher studies, startup, family business, not interested in placement

A student who opts out of placements must formally inform both the Program Office and the Placement Office by email. Once opted out, the student cannot take part in any future placement drive, so the decision is final.


### I.3.5 | Adani Group employees in the PGDM
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** SIP Policy 2025-27 §9
**Questions:** I am an Adani Group employee, can I sit for placements? | Will the Institute arrange SIP for Adani employees?
**Keywords:** Adani employee, sponsored student, working at Adani, IJP

Adani Group employees enrolled in the PGDM do not take part in placement drives. On request the Institute will arrange a SIP with a non-Adani organisation, but doing the SIP in their own Adani Group company is preferred. The Institute does not facilitate their Capstone or placement, and does not intervene in internal job postings (IJP) or other employment processes.


### I.3.6 | Institute authority and the student consent declaration
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** SIP Policy 2025-27 §10-11
**Questions:** Do I need to sign the placement policy? | What does the placement declaration say?
**Keywords:** placement policy consent, declaration, disciplinary action placement, final decision

AIDTM may amend or interpret the placement policy at any time and take disciplinary action for violations, and its decisions are final and binding. Each student signs a declaration with their name, enrollment number, programme and date. In it they confirm that they have understood the combined SIP, Capstone and Placement Policy, will follow its rules and timelines, and accept that violations can have academic or placement consequences.


### I.3.7 | Placement process, eligibility and preparation
**Type:** procedure · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Helpdesk policy written for this project (not issued by AIDTM); five-opportunity limit, offer, 1.5x and dress rules from SIP Policy and Handbook (official)
**Questions:** What is the placement process? | Who is eligible for placements? | What CGPA do I need for placements? | Does missing an interview count? | Is there placement training?
**Keywords:** placement process, placement eligibility, CV, pre placement talk, mock interview, placement training

**Process:**

| Step | What happens | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| 1. Register | Sign the SIP and Placement Policy declaration | Official, I.3.6 |
| 2. CV | Upload a one-page CV in the Institute template for Placement Office approval | Helpdesk |
| 3. Pre-placement talks | Companies present roles, CTC and process; attendance expected | Helpdesk |
| 4. Apply | Apply within the deadline on the placement portal | Helpdesk |
| 5. Selection | Tests, group discussions and interviews; each counts towards your five facilitated opportunities | Official limit of five, I.3.1 |
| 6. Offer | Accept within the deadline; silence counts as rejection; you must then join | Official, I.3.2 |

**Eligibility for final placements:**

- CGPA of at least 2.60.
- No pending F or I grade.
- At least 70% attendance in the current trimester.
- No disciplinary finding and no outstanding dues.

The Programme Chair can allow a student below a threshold to take part.

**Conduct rules:**

- Missing a confirmed interview without informing the Placement Office counts as one used opportunity.
- Wear formal dress with a blazer (official, I.4.3).
- On scheduled placement days, placement activity takes priority over classes, and attendance is adjusted.

**Preparation:** SIP Preparation (Trimester II) and the placement-readiness programme in Trimesters IV-V cover aptitude tests, case interviews, group-discussion practice, mock interviews with alumni and industry panels, and domain refreshers.


### I.3.8 | Placement season timeline for Batch 2025-27
**Type:** table · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Helpdesk policy written for this project (not issued by AIDTM); SIP duration and PPO rule from SIP Policy 2025-27 (official)

All periods except the official rules noted are indicative. The Placement Office publishes exact dates each season.

# Part J — Conduct and discipline

Academic integrity and plagiarism, phones, classroom and campus conduct, ragging, and how misconduct cases are decided and appealed.


### J.0 | Part J at a glance: conduct, discipline and penalties
**Type:** overview · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** OFFICIAL-DERIVED · **Source:** Consolidated from J.1.1 to J.1.3 (Student Handbook §12-14, Annexures VI-VIII, XVII)
**Questions:** Tell me all the discipline rules | What can get me expelled? | What are the penalties for misconduct?
**Keywords:** everything about discipline, conduct rules guide, what gets you expelled, penalties list, misconduct explained

**Academic integrity.** All submitted work must be your own; collaboration only when the instructor allows it; cite every source (APA recommended); run a plagiarism checker. Plagiarism includes copying, close paraphrasing, internet text, unattributed statistics or images, a classmate's notes, and self-plagiarism. Penalties: a penalty grade from the faculty (zero to failing the course), or Disciplinary Committee action up to expulsion; mercy petition to the Dean possible.

**Exams.** Cheating is handled by the unfair-means procedure (E.0), with penalties up to expulsion.

**Classroom and campus rules.** Phones off or silent; use in or around class brings a INR 500 spot fine and confiscation (INR 2,000 / INR 5,000 to return, permanent on the third time). No canvassing for grades; proxy attendance is severely punished; mass bunking and irresponsible behaviour are dealt with severely. Keep case data confidential. Carry your ID card always; semi-formal dress, formal on designated days; no smoking or tobacco; no use of Institute telephones; keep the campus clean. Students may not run businesses, consult for pay or take honoraria during the programme, and must not deal directly with staff or contract workers (complain to the administrative head). Institute lectures are compulsory even without credit.

**Ragging.** Banned everywhere and a criminal offence; the Anti-Ragging Committee can file an FIR; punishments include admission cancellation, suspension, scholarship withdrawal, exam bar, withheld results, hostel expulsion, rustication, expulsion and a INR 25,000 fine, with collective punishment if offenders are not identified. The anti-ragging undertaking from student and parent is compulsory before attending classes.

**How cases are decided.** Faculty decide academic breaches first and may refer to the Programme Chair and then the Academic or Disciplinary Committee; non-academic cases go to the Programme Chair and serious ones to the Disciplinary Committee, whose decision is binding. A mercy appeal to the Dean is the final step. A student expelled for misconduct cannot seek re-admission.


## J.1 Academic integrity


### J.1.1 | Academic integrity and collaboration
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** Student Handbook §12; Annexure VI
**Questions:** Can I work with friends on assignments? | What is academic integrity? | Do I need to cite sources?
**Keywords:** academic integrity, collaboration on assignment, copying assignment, honesty

All submitted work must be the student's own. Unless the instructor allows it, students must not collaborate on home assignments and projects. Every source of information or ideas must be acknowledged, and the unreferenced part of a submission must be the student's independent work. Discussing ideas with teachers and classmates is encouraged, but reliance on others' work must always be credited.


### J.1.2 | Plagiarism: types and penalties
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** Student Handbook §12.2; Annexure VI
**Questions:** What is plagiarism? | What is self-plagiarism? | What is the penalty for plagiarism? | Can I appeal a plagiarism penalty?
**Keywords:** plagiarism, self plagiarism, copied from internet, penalty for plagiarism, mercy petition

Plagiarism means using another person's ideas, processes, results or words without credit. It can be accidental, for example confusing quoting with paraphrasing, or blatant, for example submitting borrowed or bought work. Self-plagiarism, meaning resubmitting substantially the same work in two courses without permission, also counts, and students are held to the same standard whether or not they meant to plagiarise. Examples: copying text word for word, close paraphrasing, internet text, or unacknowledged statistics, figures, images or a classmate's notes. Faculty can give a penalty grade, up to failing the assignment or course. The Disciplinary Committee can impose anything from zero in the component to expulsion, and its decision is final. A student may file a mercy petition with the Dean.


### J.1.3 | Academic integrity: full definitions and examples of plagiarism
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** Student Handbook Annexure VI
**Questions:** What counts as plagiarism? | Give examples of plagiarism | What is a penalty grade?
**Keywords:** what is academic integrity, what counts as plagiarism, plagiarism examples, penalty grade, original work policy

Academic integrity means creating and expressing your own ideas, acknowledging all sources, completing work independently or acknowledging collaboration, reporting results accurately, and being honest in exams. Policy statement: all homework, projects, reports, papers, theses and exams submitted for credit must be the student's own. "Sources" includes print, online and information or opinions obtained directly from people. Submitting others' work, or work without clear attribution, can lead to disciplinary action up to a requirement to withdraw. Plagiarism (from the Latin plagiarius, "abductor") is the appropriation of another person's ideas, processes, results or words without credit. Examples include copying word for word or paraphrasing very closely, using downloaded internet text, or borrowing statistics or facts without acknowledgement. Copying figures, photos or diagrams without credit, copying a classmate's notes or essays, or copying notes that contain direct quotations also count. A faculty member may give a "penalty grade", defined as a grade assigned on a charge of academic misconduct, which can include failing the assignment or the course. Reusing coursework in another class without permission can bring an allegation of academic dishonesty.


### J.1.4 | Referencing style and plagiarism checking
**Type:** reference · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** Student Handbook Annexure VI
**Questions:** Which referencing style should I use? | Where can I check my work for plagiarism? | Which APA guides are recommended?
**Keywords:** APA, citation style, referencing, plagiarism checker

AIDTM encourages the APA (American Psychological Association) referencing style. Recommended guides include the Purdue OWL APA guide, the Cardiff University APA tutorial and the UW-Madison Writing Center APA documentation. Students should run a plagiarism checker before submitting any assignment or project. Free checkers suggested in the Handbook: thepensters.com free plagiarism checker, plagiarisma.net and plagscan.com.


## J.2 Campus and classroom conduct


### J.2.1 | Mobile phone policy
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** Student Handbook §12.3; Annexure VII
**Questions:** Can I use my phone in class? | What happens if my phone is confiscated? | How much is the fine to get my phone back?
**Keywords:** mobile phone in class, phone confiscated, phone fine, get phone back, camera on campus

Mobile phones must be switched off or on silent in class, and using them in or around classrooms and the foyer is prohibited, with a spot fine of INR 500 possible. A phone that rings or is used in class may be confiscated and deposited at the Program Office. It is returned after a fine of INR 2,000 the first time or INR 5,000 the second time. On a third violation the phone is confiscated permanently and the Disciplinary Committee acts. The Institute may inspect the data on a phone confiscated during an exam. Using a phone camera on campus for any undesired purpose is prohibited.


### J.2.2 | Other academic norms: proxy attendance and canvassing for grades
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** Student Handbook §12.4
**Questions:** What happens if I mark proxy attendance? | Can I ask a teacher to increase my grade? | Is mass bunking allowed?
**Keywords:** proxy attendance, canvassing grades, mass bunk, case data confidentiality

Canvassing for grades is prohibited. Marking proxy attendance, or having attendance marked by someone else, is severely punished. Mass abstention from classes, irresponsible behaviour, unethical practices during internships or placements, and other rule violations are dealt with severely. The confidentiality of case and business data used in teaching must be protected.


### J.2.3 | Non-academic misconduct
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** Student Handbook §13
**Questions:** Can I smoke on campus? | Can I do a part-time job or freelancing during the PGDM? | How do I complain about a staff member?
**Keywords:** smoking, tobacco, part time job, freelancing, commercial work, misbehaviour, staff complaint

Students must behave respectfully on and off campus, and disrespect can lead to penalties from a fine up to expulsion. Smoking and chewing tobacco are prohibited on campus. Students may not run any commercial venture or advise organisations on a paid basis or receive an honorarium during their studies. They must not deal directly with Institute employees or contract workers; complaints about staff go to the relevant administrative head. Lectures organised by the Institute are compulsory, even though they carry no credit.


### J.2.4 | General campus rules and dress code
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** Student Handbook Annexure XVII
**Questions:** What are the general campus rules? | What is the dress code? | Do I need to carry my ID card all the time?
**Keywords:** campus rules, ID card always, dress code, telephone, cleanliness

Students must carry their ID card on campus at all times and keep the campus clean. Institute telephones may not be used for any purpose. Proxy attendance, unfair exam practices and unacknowledged material invite disciplinary action. The dress code is semi-formal, with formal dress on designated days and a blazer during placements. In class, students follow each faculty member's norms, maintain discipline and mutual respect, and come prepared with the study material.


## J.3 Ragging


### J.3.1 | Anti-ragging rules and punishments
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** All AIDTM students · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** Student Handbook §13.1; Annexure VIII
**Questions:** What is the punishment for ragging? | What should I do if I am ragged? | Is the anti-ragging undertaking compulsory?
**Keywords:** ragging, harassment by seniors, FIR, anti ragging undertaking, punishment for ragging

Ragging in any form, anywhere, is banned and is a criminal offence. Where there is a prima facie case, the Anti-Ragging Committee acts immediately, which can include filing an FIR with the local police. Punishments include any combination of the following: cancelling admission; suspension from classes; withholding scholarships or other benefits; barring from exams; withholding results; barring from representing the institution; suspension or expulsion from the hostel; rustication; expulsion; and a fine of INR 25,000. Collective punishment may apply if the offenders cannot be identified. Every student and parent must submit an anti-ragging undertaking at the start of the session, or the student cannot attend classes.


### J.3.2 | Background of the anti-ragging rules and mandatory FIR
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** All AIDTM students · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** Student Handbook Annexure VIII
**Questions:** Why are the anti-ragging rules so strict? | When must the Institute file an FIR for ragging?
**Keywords:** Raghavan committee, Supreme Court ragging, FIR mandatory, ragging free campus

AIDTM's anti-ragging rules follow the Raghavan Committee recommendations accepted by the Supreme Court of India (SLP No. 24295/2006), which call for exemplary and justifiably harsh punishment. If the victim, their parent or guardian, or the Head of Institution is not satisfied with the institutional action in any ragging incident, the Institute must file an FIR with the local police. Failure or deliberate delay in doing so counts as culpable negligence. Ragging cases are handled on priority, and the Anti-Ragging Committee is headed by a senior faculty member. The Institute aims for a "Ragging Free AIDTM".


## J.4 How cases are decided


### J.4.1 | How misconduct is handled and the right to appeal
**Type:** procedure · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** Student Handbook §14
**Questions:** How is misconduct handled? | Who decides disciplinary action? | Can I appeal a disciplinary decision? | Can an expelled student be re-admitted?
**Keywords:** disciplinary action, penalty, expulsion, mercy appeal, disciplinary committee decision

Penalties range from a warning to expulsion depending on severity. Academic breaches are first decided by the faculty member, who may refer them to the Programme Chair and then the Academic or Disciplinary Committee. Non-academic cases go to the Programme Chair, and serious ones are referred to the Institute Disciplinary Committee, whose decision is binding and conveyed by the Programme Chair. A student may make a mercy appeal to the Dean, who decides in consultation with the instructor and the Academic Committee, and the Dean's decision is final. A student expelled for misconduct cannot seek re-admission.
