# Part K — Facilities

Library, computer centre and IT, classrooms, recreation, printing, and what to do in a medical emergency.


### K.0 | Part K at a glance: facilities
**Type:** overview · **Applies to:** All AIDTM students · **Status:** OFFICIAL-DERIVED · **Source:** Consolidated from K.1.1 to K.1.2 (Student Handbook §15, Annexures IX-XII, XVIII; Late Fees Policy v02)
**Questions:** Tell me about all the facilities | What facilities does AIDTM have? | Where do I go in an emergency?
**Keywords:** everything about facilities, library guide, campus facilities explained, lab rules, where to go in emergency

**Library.** Hours 9:30 AM-6:30 PM Monday to Friday and 9:30 AM-1:30 PM Saturday; circulation 10 AM-6 PM and 10 AM-1 PM. Free membership; KOHA system; Zotero; book bank. Borrow 3 books for 14 days, one renewal; INR 5 per day overdue (up to 60 days); reference books, CDs/DVDs, project reports, journals and magazines are not lent; report lost books at once, replace with the latest edition within 15 days plus fines; unreported after 90 days means lost and you pay the higher of MRP or 3x the price. Scan your ID at entry and exit; only notebooks and library books inside; silence, no food, no sleeping; internet for study only; photography needs permission. Assistant Librarian: Mr. Chetas Bhagat.

**Computer Centre.** Intel i5 Dell desktops, 100 Mbps line, Cisco network, Wi-Fi, Office 365, MSDN, Visual Studio. Entry with I-Card only, not during lectures; Program Office credentials for login and Institute credentials for internet; no pen drives or discs; no phones, food or drink; shut down properly; damage is charged. IT Officer: Mr. Jignesh Parekh.

**Classrooms and recreation.** Classrooms have video conferencing, flip boards, face-recognition attendance and sound systems; wear your I-Card; no food; no pen drives on flip boards; damage is recovered. The recreation facility is open outside lecture hours; sign out sports equipment and return it; water only in the sports room; switch off screens when leaving.

**Stationery and printing.** Not provided by the Institute.

**Medical.** Call 108 for an ambulance; inform Reception 079-25558180 (ext. 201) or the Program Office 9099981586. Nearby hospitals: Sanjivani (Adalaj) 9376123616; Amba Health Centre (Adalaj) 9313883377; KD Hospital 079-66770000; Civil Hospital Sola 079-27664355; Zydus 8238690695 / 8000565681; Shalby 079-40203265 / 9904422899; HCG Medi-surge 079-40010101.


## K.1 Library


### K.1.1 | Library: overview and opening hours
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** All AIDTM students · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** Student Handbook §15.1
**Questions:** What are the library timings? | Is the library open on Saturday? | What is KOHA? | Is library membership free?
**Keywords:** library timings, library hours, KOHA, Zotero, library membership, book bank

The library has books, journals, magazines, newspapers, student reports, audiovisual and online resources on management, analytics, AI and data science. It runs on the KOHA library management system, and Zotero is available for reference management. Membership is free for enrolled students, and a book bank facility is available. Library hours are 9:30 AM to 6:30 PM Monday to Friday and 9:30 AM to 1:30 PM on Saturday. Circulation hours (issue and return) are 10:00 AM to 6:00 PM Monday to Friday and 10:00 AM to 1:00 PM on Saturday. The library and reading room cannot be used during a student's lecture hours. The Assistant Librarian is Mr. Chetas Bhagat.


### K.1.2 | Library collection and memberships
**Type:** profile · **Applies to:** All AIDTM students · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** Student Handbook §15.1
**Questions:** What books and journals does the library have? | Which subjects does the library cover?
**Keywords:** library books subjects, journals, e-resources, library membership free

The centrally located library and reading room hold books, journals, magazines, newspapers, student reports, audiovisual materials and online resources. Subjects covered: Economics, Banking and Finance, Business Management, Marketing, Python, R Programming, Machine Learning, MIS, Big Data, Data Analytics, Technology Management, and AI and Data Science. Membership is free for regular and visiting faculty, students, staff and stakeholders on enrolment. The library has organisational memberships with other academic institutions to extend access. It runs on KOHA, with Zotero available to all users.


### K.1.3 | Library borrowing limits and overdue fines
**Type:** table · **Applies to:** All AIDTM students · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** Late Fees Policy v02 §2; Student Handbook Annexure IX
**Questions:** How many books can I borrow? | For how many days can I borrow a book? | What is the library fine? | Can I renew a book?
**Keywords:** how many books, borrow books, loan period, renew book, overdue fine, library fine

All AIDTM students and employees have access to the library facilities and services. Students can borrow up to 3 books for 14 days each, with one renewal. The overdue fine is INR 5 per day per book, for up to 60 days. Books are issued only against the student's own, non-transferable ID card; proxy borrowing is not allowed, but anyone may return a book on the borrower's behalf. Reference books, CDs/DVDs and project reports are for in-library use only, and journals and magazines cannot be borrowed. The library can recall a book or shorten a loan in exceptional circumstances. Unpaid fines are adjusted from the caution deposit.


### K.1.4 | Lost, damaged or unreturned library books
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** All AIDTM students · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** Late Fees Policy v02 §2 (prevails over Handbook Annexure IX)
**Questions:** What if I lose a library book? | What if I damage a library book? | When is a book treated as lost?
**Keywords:** lost book, damaged book, torn book, replace book, book not returned

Report a lost or damaged book immediately, because overdue fines keep accruing until it is reported. After reporting, the borrower must replace it with the latest edition within 15 days and pay all overdue fines up to the reporting date. If a lost book is later found and returned, fines run up to the actual return date. A book neither returned nor reported within 90 days of its due date is treated as lost. The borrower then pays the higher of the latest edition's MRP or three times the original price. Fair charges for a torn or badly kept book are at the librarian's discretion.


### K.1.5 | Library and reading room code of conduct
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** All AIDTM students · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** Student Handbook Annexure IX
**Questions:** What are the library rules? | Can I take my bag into the library? | Can I eat in the library?
**Keywords:** library rules, silence, food in library, bags in library, photography

Students must scan their ID at the entry and exit gate and produce it on demand. Only notebooks and library books are allowed inside, and bags and personal items go to the property counter; the library is not responsible for lost belongings. Students must keep silent, silence their phones, and not bring food or drinks, loiter or sleep. Leave used books on a table rather than shelving them. Internet in the library is for educational use only, photography needs authorisation, and furniture must not be rearranged. Breaking these rules can lead to suspension or cancellation of membership. Feedback can be given via the QR code at the help desk.


## K.2 Computer centre and IT


### K.2.1 | Computer Centre facilities and rules
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** All AIDTM students · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** Student Handbook §15.2; Annexure X
**Questions:** What computers are in the lab? | Can I use a pen drive in the lab? | How do I log in to lab computers? | What is the internet speed?
**Keywords:** computer lab, lab rules, pen drive, internet login, WiFi, lab credentials

The Computer Centre has Intel Core i5 Dell desktops, a 100 Mbps leased internet line, Cisco fibre networking, Wi-Fi, Windows Server 10 Pro with Microsoft Office 365, Microsoft MSDN and Visual Studio. Entry is not allowed during lecture hours, and an I-Card is required. Students log in with credentials issued by the Program Office, and internet access works only with Institute credentials. Pen drives, CDs and DVDs may not be connected. Phones, gadgets, food and drink are not allowed. Students must shut systems down properly and pay for any damage they cause. The IT Officer is Mr. Jignesh Parekh.


### K.2.2 | IT accounts, Wi-Fi and acceptable use
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** All AIDTM students · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Helpdesk policy written for this project (not issued by AIDTM); credential and device rules from Student Handbook Annexure X (official)
**Questions:** How do I connect to campus Wi-Fi? | How many devices can I connect? | I forgot my password, what do I do? | Can I use a VPN or torrents on campus? | Is my internet use monitored?
**Keywords:** wifi, internet, password reset, IT account, acceptable use, VPN, torrent, monitoring, devices

**Accounts:**

- One Institute account (email, student portal, LMS and Wi-Fi) is issued at registration. Lab and internet credentials are issued by the Program Office (official).
- Passwords must be at least 10 characters, changed every 90 days and never shared. Multi-factor authentication is required for email.

**Wi-Fi:** sign in with your Institute credentials. Up to 2 personal devices per student (laptop and phone) can be registered.

**Password reset:** visit the IT Officer (Mr. Jignesh Parekh) with your ID card, or raise an "IT & Login" request. Staff never ask for your password (N.1.3).

**Not allowed:**

- Sharing credentials.
- Torrents and pirated software or media.
- Using VPNs or proxies to bypass Institute filters.
- Port scanning or hacking outside supervised lab exercises.
- Crypto mining.
- Accessing or sharing offensive material.
- Connecting external drives to lab machines (official).

**Monitoring:** network use is logged for security.

**Penalties:** misuse leads to suspension of access and action under J.4.1.

**Software:** Microsoft 365 is provided through the Institute account. Course software is listed in D.4.4.

Classrooms have video-conferencing with motion-tracking cameras, multi-touch flip boards, face-recognition (FR) attendance devices and sound systems. # Part L — Hostel and campus life

Hostel rooms, fees and rules, the mess, transport, safety on campus, clubs, awards, convocation and alumni.


### L.0 | Part L at a glance: hostel and campus life
**Type:** overview · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Consolidated from L.1.2 to L.1.5 and O.1.1 to D.4.2, M.3.1 to M.2.4 (sample helpdesk policies; the programme being residential and hostel/mess being outside the fee are official)
**Questions:** Tell me everything about the hostel | Explain hostel, mess and transport | What is campus life like?
**Keywords:** everything about hostel, hostel guide, mess and transport explained, student services guide, campus life rules

**Hostel.** Twin-sharing AC INR 1,50,000 a year; triple-sharing non-AC INR 1,00,000; refundable hostel deposit INR 10,000; paid in two installments with tuition (15 July and 15 January), late-fee slabs apply. Charges cover room, electricity, water, housekeeping, Wi-Fi and laundry pick-up. In-time 10:30 PM with biometric entry; overnight leave via the warden's register; visitors only in the common area 10 AM-7 PM; no smoking, alcohol, drugs, cooking appliances or pets; one room change a year; damage charged to the room's residents; ragging leads to hostel expulsion.

**Mess.** INR 60,000 a year (INR 5,000 a month); breakfast 7:30-9:00, lunch 12:30-2:00, tea 5:00-5:30, dinner 7:30-9:00; vegetarian with egg at breakfast; compulsory for residents; day scholars can buy a INR 4,000 monthly card; refunds only for approved absences of 15 days or more.

**Transport and parking.** Shuttle from Ahmedabad and Gandhinagar pick-up points INR 24,000 a year, leaving 8:15 AM and returning 6:00 PM, half day on Saturday; free parking with a vehicle pass.

**Admissions and registration.** Graduation with 50% (45% reserved categories) and a valid CAT/XAT/MAT/CMAT/ATMA/GMAT score; interview and written test; seat confirmed with the INR 2,00,000 registration fee. Submit mark sheets, degree, scorecard, photo ID, photographs, category and medical certificates, the placement policy declaration and the anti-ragging undertaking.

**ID, email, portal and classes.** Enrollment number, ID card and Institute email at registration (duplicate ID INR 500); the portal shows timetable, attendance, grades, fees and requests; classes 9:30 AM-5:30 PM Monday to Friday in four 90-minute sessions with Saturday mornings for visiting faculty and labs; a laptop with i5, 16 GB RAM and 512 GB SSD is required.

**Services.** Group medical insurance of INR 2,00,000 a year; a weekly visiting counsellor booked confidentially via the Program Office; clubs (Placement Committee, Analytics & AI Club, Cultural, Sports, Alumni Cell); convocation tentatively June 2027 with provisional certificates two weeks after results; sexual-harassment complaints to the WGRC within three months, acknowledged in 7 days, inquiry within 90 days.


## L.1 Hostel, mess and transport


### L.1.1 | Hostel, accommodation and mess
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** Admission Offer Annexure II; Student Handbook Annexure III
**Questions:** Is the PGDM residential? | Is hostel included in the programme fee? | Whom do I contact about the hostel?
**Keywords:** hostel, accommodation, mess, food charges, room, residential

The PGDM is described as a fully residential programme. Accommodation and mess charges are not included in the programme fee and are billed separately. AIDTM's official hostel and mess tariff was not provided; sample hostel fees, mess charges and hostel rules built for this project are in L.1.2 to L.1.5. For official figures, contact the Program Office or Operations (Head - Operations: Mr. Ritesh Soni).


### L.1.2 | Hostel rooms, fees and what is included
**Type:** table · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Helpdesk policy written for this project (not issued by AIDTM) (official AIDTM hostel tariff not provided; hostel is outside the programme fee per Admission Offer Annexure II)
**Questions:** How much is the hostel fee? | What types of hostel rooms are there? | What is included in the hostel fee? | Is there a hostel deposit? | Do I pay hostel fees during the internship?
**Keywords:** hostel fee, room rent, accommodation charges, hostel deposit, room types, AC room

| Room type | Fee per academic year (INR) | Payment |
|---|---|---|
| Twin-sharing, air-conditioned | 1,50,000 | Two equal installments, due 15 July and 15 January, with tuition |
| Triple-sharing, non-AC | 1,00,000 | Same as above |
| Hostel caution deposit | 10,000, paid once and refundable | With the first hostel payment |

**Included:** furnished room (bed, mattress, study table, chair, wardrobe), electricity, water, housekeeping of common areas, Wi-Fi, and laundry pick-up twice a week. Laundry charges are extra.

**Not included:** mess charges (L.1.4) and personal items.

**Late payment:** the Late Fees Policy slabs (F.3.1) also apply to hostel installments.

**SIP period:** rooms are vacated before the Summer Internship. Hostel fees are not charged for the SIP months, and a storage room is available for belongings.

**Leaving mid-year:** the current half-year's fee is not refundable, and the next half-year is not charged. The deposit is refunded after the room inspection, less any damage.

**Allotment:** by the Operations team at registration. Requests (for example a roommate preference) are considered but not guaranteed.


### L.1.3 | Hostel rules
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Helpdesk policy written for this project (not issued by AIDTM); ragging rules official (Student Handbook Annexure VIII)

| Rooms | Kept clean; monthly inspection; one room change per year with the warden's approval |

Serious breaches go to the Disciplinary Committee (J.4.1). The warden can be contacted through Operations (Head - Operations: Mr. Ritesh Soni).

| Evening tea | 5:00 PM to 5:30 PM |
| Dinner | 7:30 PM to 9:00 PM |

**Menu:** vegetarian, with an egg option at breakfast, on a weekly rotation set by the student mess committee. Jain food and a light "sick diet" are available on request, a day in advance.

**Refunds:** given only for approved absences of 15 or more consecutive days (for example SIP or long medical leave), pro-rated per day.

**Feedback:** hygiene and quality feedback through the mess committee, or a "Hostel & Facilities" support request.


### L.1.5 | Campus transport and parking
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Sample policy built for this project
**Questions:** Is there a college bus? | How much is the transport fee? | What are the shuttle timings? | Is parking available?
**Keywords:** bus, transport, shuttle, parking, day scholar commute

Day scholars can use the Institute shuttle from designated pick-up points in Ahmedabad and Gandhinagar for INR 24,000 per academic year, paid in two installments with tuition. Shuttles leave at 8:15 AM and return at 6:00 PM, with a Saturday half-day service. Two-wheeler and car parking is free in the marked area with a vehicle pass issued by Operations; vehicles are parked at the owner's risk.


## L.2 Campus life


### L.2.1 | Clubs, committees and student activities
**Type:** profile · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Helpdesk policy written for this project (not issued by AIDTM); student-driven activities and awards are official (Student Handbook §15.3, §16.6)

Student activities are student-driven, and the Institute supports initiatives that add value (official, Student Handbook §15.3).

| Body | What it does |
|---|---|
| Student Placement Committee | Assists the Placement Office with company coordination, pre-placement talks and logistics |
| Analytics & AI Club | Hackathons, Kaggle sessions, paper-reading groups, tool workshops, guest talks |

**Joining:** each body recruits in the first month of Trimester I and again in Trimester IV.

**Starting a new club:** at least 5 members, a faculty advisor and a one-page proposal to the Program Office.

**Events and budgets:** events need approval at least 7 days in advance, and budgets go through the Program Office.

**Recognition:** participation counts towards the Annual Academic Awards (L.2.3).

- Academic areas have HD CCTV (official).

**Visitors:** register at the gate with photo ID. Visitors may meet students in the reception and common areas.

**Lost and found:** at Reception (079-25558180). Report a lost ID card at once (D.4.2).

| Ambulance | 108 |
| Police | 100 |
| Fire | 101 |

| Program Office | 9099981586 |

**Harassment:** report it to the Women Grievance Redressal Committee (M.2.4) or any committee member.

**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** All AIDTM students · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** Student Handbook §16.6

AIDTM holds an annual Academic Award Function to honour students for strong performance in academics, sports, cultural and other institutional activities.


### L.2.4 | Convocation and alumni
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Sample policy built for this project; award criteria are official (Handbook §11)
**Questions:** When is the convocation? | When will I get my diploma? | What alumni benefits are there?
**Keywords:** convocation, diploma certificate, alumni network, alumni card, when do I get my diploma

Convocation for Batch 2025-27 is tentatively planned for June 2027. Students who meet the award criteria (CGPA 2.17 or above, no F or I, no dues, capstone complete) receive the PGDM and the AIHER Diploma in Strategic Management. Provisional certificates are issued within two weeks of results, and the final diploma at convocation. Graduates join the AIDTM alumni network, receive an alumni email and card, and can use the library and attend placement talks as alumni. Alumni are invited to mentor current students and recruit from campus.

# Part M — Student support and grievances

Raising and tracking support requests, escalation, the formal grievance process, committees, sexual-harassment complaints, and wellbeing and insurance.


### M.0 | Part M at a glance: support, grievances and privacy
**Type:** overview · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** OFFICIAL-DERIVED · **Source:** Consolidated from M.2.1 to L.2.3 (official) and M.1.1 to M.1.3, N.1.1 to N.1.5 (helpdesk policies)
**Questions:** How do I get help? | Explain the support and grievance process | What can the helpdesk do for me?
**Keywords:** how to get help, support guide, grievance and complaint guide, privacy rules explained, what can the helpdesk do

**Raising a request (helpdesk).** Sign in and raise a support request in one of these categories: Fees & Payments (Accounts); Scholarships; Academics, Grades & Attendance; Examinations; Course Change; Certificates & Documents; IT & Login; Library; SIP & Placements; Hostel & Facilities; General Grievance. Each gets an ID like SR-2026-0001. Urgent requests are acknowledged within 4 working hours, High within 1 working day, Normal within 3 working days and resolved within 7. Statuses: Open, In Progress, Awaiting Student, Pending Approval, Resolved, Closed, Escalated. Ragging and harassment complaints go directly to the committees; medical emergencies are never tickets (call 108).

**Escalation and formal grievance (official timelines).** Escalate to the department head, then the Programme Chair, then the Student Grievance Redressal Committee: the Chairperson puts the case to the Committee within 15 days, the decision is communicated within 7 days, an appeal to the Dean is possible within one week, and the Dean decides within 15 days. Committee members are listed in M.2.2; the Dean chairs the SGRC/WGRC and Anti-Ragging Committees.

**Privacy (helpdesk).** You can see only your own record (fees, scholarship, grades, attendance, requests, contact details). The helpdesk will not disclose another student's data to anyone, including classmates, the CR or someone claiming to be a parent; parents get information only through the registered contact with your written consent. Every access and change is logged with who, when, what, the decision and the result, and you can ask for the log of your own record.

**Sensitive changes need approval (helpdesk).** Changing your registered mobile, email or address, your refund bank account, a name or date-of-birth correction, a programme switch, withdrawing a scholarship application, or opting out of placements (which is final) is executed only after identity verification and approval by the Program Office; a rejected request changes nothing.

**Secrets.** Staff and the helpdesk never ask for OTPs, passwords, PINs, CVVs or full card or account numbers; never type them in chat; if shared by mistake they are not stored, and you should change the password and contact the IT Officer. The helpdesk remembers only durable, non-sensitive preferences, and you can ask it to forget them.


## M.1 The student helpdesk


### M.1.1 | Support requests: categories, who handles them and examples
**Type:** table · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Helpdesk policy written for this project (not issued by AIDTM)
**Questions:** How do I raise a support request or ticket? | Which category should I choose? | Who handles my request? | Can I raise a ticket for hostel or Wi-Fi problems?
**Keywords:** raise ticket, support request, helpdesk, complaint ticket, categories, who handles

Sign in to the student helpdesk, describe the issue, and the request is created with an ID such as SR-2026-0101. You can also raise one in chat ("raise a ticket: …").

| Category | Handled by | Typical examples |
|---|---|---|
| Fees & Payments | Accounts | Payment failed, receipt missing, fee demand letter |
| Scholarships | Program Office with Accounts | Application, missing document, status query |
| Academics, Grades & Attendance | Program Office | Attendance discrepancy, grade query, I grade |
| Examinations | Program Office | Hall ticket, exam clash, seating |
| Course Change | Program Office / Academic Committee | Elective add or drop, programme switch |
| Certificates & Documents | Program Office / Placement Office | Bonafide, transcript, NOC |
| IT & Login | IT Officer | Password reset, Wi-Fi, LMS access |
| Library | Assistant Librarian | Fine query, lost book |
| SIP & Placements | Placement Office | SIP approval, offer letter, placement eligibility |
| Hostel & Facilities | Operations | Room repair, mess, AC, water |
| General Grievance | Student Grievance Redressal Committee | Anything unresolved or unfair treatment |

**Not handled through tickets:**

- Ragging: contact the Anti-Ragging Committee directly (J.3.1).
- Sexual harassment: contact the WGRC directly (M.2.4).
- Medical emergency: call 108 (K.4.1).


### M.1.2 | Support request priority, response times and status
**Type:** table · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Sample helpdesk procedure written for this project
**Questions:** How long will my ticket take? | What do the ticket statuses mean? | What are the helpdesk working hours?
**Keywords:** ticket status, how long will it take, SLA, response time, open, resolved, escalated

Priority and target times: Urgent (safety, or an issue on the day of an exam) is acknowledged within 4 working hours. High (a payment failure near a due date, or a deadline within 3 days) is acknowledged within 1 working day. Normal requests are acknowledged within 3 working days and resolved within 7 working days. Working hours are 9:30 AM to 6:00 PM Monday to Friday and 9:30 AM to 1:30 PM Saturday, excluding Institute holidays. Statuses: Open, In Progress, Awaiting Student, Pending Approval, Resolved, Closed, Escalated. A student can view and update only their own requests.


### M.1.3 | Escalating an unresolved request
**Type:** procedure · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Helpdesk policy written for this project (not issued by AIDTM); levels 3-4 follow the official grievance process (Student Handbook §16)
**Questions:** My ticket is not resolved, what can I do? | How do I escalate a complaint? | Whom do I escalate to after the Programme Chair?
**Keywords:** escalate, no response, complaint not resolved, unhappy with resolution, grievance escalation

| Level | When to use it | Whom | Expected response |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Request past its target time (M.1.2) | Reply on the ticket; it is marked Escalated and goes to the head of the office concerned | 2 working days |
| 2 | Still unresolved, or you disagree with the outcome | Programme Chair (Dr. Ankit Shah) by email | 5 working days |
| 3 | Unresolved after level 2, or a matter of unfair treatment | Formal grievance to the Chairperson of the Student Grievance Redressal Committee (M.2.1) | Official timelines: committee within 15 days, decision communicated within 7 days |
| 4 | Not satisfied with the committee's action | Written appeal to the Dean within one week | Dean's decision within 15 days; final |

Keep the ticket ID and any emails, and attach them at each level.


## M.2 Grievances and committees


### M.2.1 | Formal grievance redressal process and timelines
**Type:** procedure · **Applies to:** All AIDTM students · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** Student Handbook §16, §16.4
**Questions:** How do I file a formal grievance? | How long does the grievance committee take? | Can I appeal to the Dean?
**Keywords:** grievance, complaint, how to complain, grievance timeline, appeal to Dean

A student can approach any faculty member, the Program Office, the Programme Chair or the Dean informally. The formal process works as follows. (1) Call or write to the Chairperson of the Student Grievance Committee. (2) The Chairperson investigates and puts the case to the Committee within 15 days. (3) The decision is communicated to the student within 7 days of the Committee's decision. (4) If unsatisfied, the student may write to the Dean within one week of being told the outcome. (5) The Dean hears all parties and communicates a final decision within 15 days of the appeal.


### M.2.2 | Committees and their members
**Type:** reference · **Applies to:** All AIDTM students · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** Student Handbook Annexures XIII-XVI
**Questions:** Who is on the grievance committee? | Who are the anti-ragging committee members? | Who chairs the discipline committee?
**Keywords:** committee members, grievance committee chair, anti ragging committee contact, discipline committee, WGRC members

Academic Committee: Dr. Rama Moondra (Chair), Dr. Ankit Shah, Dr. Bhavesh Dharmani, Mr. Ritesh Soni, Mr. Vyankatesh Emche. Discipline Committee: Dr. Ankit Shah (Chair), Dr. Rama Moondra, Dr. Roshni Rawal, Mr. Vyankatesh Emche, Mr. Jay Joshi, Mr. Bhavesh Panchal. Anti-Ragging Committee: Dr. Rama Moondra (Chair), Dr. Ankit Shah, Dr. Roshni Rawal, Mr. Ritesh Soni, Ms. Anshita Mulchandani, Mr. Hiren Vinzuda. Student Grievance Redressal Committee / Women Grievance Redressal Committee: Dr. Rama Moondra (Chair), Dr. Ankit Shah, Dr. Roshni Rawal, Mr. Jay Joshi, Ms. Anshita Mulchandani, Mr. Ritesh Soni.


### M.2.3 | Roles of the Academic, Disciplinary, SGRC and WGRC committees
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** All AIDTM students · **Status:** OFFICIAL · **Source:** Student Handbook §16.1-16.5; Annexure XVI
**Questions:** What does the academic committee do? | What does the WGRC do? | What is the SGRC?
**Keywords:** sexual harassment complaint, women grievance, SGRC, academic committee role, re-assessment

The Academic Committee decides academic matters. These include eligibility for re-assessment of failed subjects, with one-on-one interactions with applicants, and improving attendance. The Disciplinary Committee decides non-academic misconduct and general discipline. The SGRC, formed under the AICTE regulations of 2012, handles personal grievances of students and parents. The WGRC handles complaints of sexual harassment from women students and staff. It examines and investigates complaints, counsels the person concerned and recommends action to the authorities, meeting twice a year and whenever needed. The Anti-Ragging Committee, formed under a Supreme Court directive, acts on ragging complaints; according to the Handbook, no ragging complaint has been received by the committee so far, and students can contact any committee member if an incident occurs.


### M.2.4 | Prevention of sexual harassment: how to complain
**Type:** procedure · **Applies to:** All AIDTM students · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** WGRC role and members are official (Handbook §16.5, Annexure XVI); complaint timelines built for this project in line with the POSH Act 2013
**Questions:** How do I report sexual harassment? | Is my complaint confidential? | How long does a harassment inquiry take?
**Keywords:** sexual harassment, POSH, internal committee, confidential complaint, WGRC process

Complaints of sexual harassment are handled by the Women Grievance Redressal Committee, chaired by Dr. Rama Moondra (official). A complaint can be made in writing or by email to the Chairperson or any member within three months of the incident (extendable for valid reasons). The Committee acknowledges the complaint within 7 days, keeps the identity of the complainant confidential, may provide interim measures such as a change of batch or hostel room, completes the inquiry within 90 days, and recommends action to the Dean. Retaliation against a complainant or witness is itself misconduct. The committee meets twice a year and whenever needed (official).


## M.3 Wellbeing


### M.3.1 | Medical insurance, counselling and mental-health support
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Helpdesk policy written for this project (not issued by AIDTM); emergency contacts from Student Handbook Annexure XVIII (official)
**Questions:** Do students have health insurance? | How do I claim medical insurance? | Is there a counsellor on campus? | Is counselling confidential? | Is there a mental health helpline?
**Keywords:** health insurance, medical insurance claim, counsellor, mental health, wellbeing, Tele-MANAS, stress

**Group medical insurance:**

- Every PGDM student is covered for hospitalisation up to INR 2,00,000 per policy year. The premium is included in the registration fee.
- Cashless treatment at network hospitals, using the insurance card issued at registration. For other hospitals, pay and claim reimbursement.
- To claim, submit the original bills, discharge summary, prescriptions and reports to the Program Office within 15 days of discharge.
- Outpatient visits, cosmetic treatment and pre-existing conditions in the first year are generally excluded, as in standard group policies. Check the policy document.

**Counselling:**

- A visiting counsellor is on campus one afternoon a week.
- Book through the Program Office or by email. Sessions are free and confidential: nothing is shared without your consent, except where there is a risk to life.
- Faculty mentors and the Programme Chair are also available for academic stress.

**If you are in distress:**

- Speak to someone you trust, or call Tele-MANAS, the national mental-health helpline: 14416 (free, 24x7).
- In an emergency, call 112 or 108.

The Institute's medical contacts and nearby hospitals are listed in K.4.1.

# Part N — Privacy, identity and data protection

Who can see your data, which changes need approval, why secrets are never asked for or stored, what the helpdesk remembers, and the audit trail.


### N.0 | Part N at a glance: your data and the helpdesk
**Type:** overview · **Applies to:** All AIDTM students · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Helpdesk policy written for this project (not issued by AIDTM)
**Questions:** How is my data protected? | Can other students see my data? | What does the helpdesk do with my information?
**Keywords:** privacy, data protection, DPDP Act, personal data, helpdesk rules

**The five privacy rules of the student helpdesk:**

1. You see only your own record (N.1.1).
2. Sensitive changes need human approval (N.1.2).
3. Secrets such as OTPs and passwords are never asked for or stored (N.1.3).
4. Only durable, non-sensitive preferences are remembered (N.1.4).
5. Every access and change is audited (N.1.5).

These rules are enforced in the helpdesk software itself, not only in its instructions. They follow the principles of India's Digital Personal Data Protection Act, 2023: purpose limitation, data minimisation and accountability.


## N.1 Privacy rules


### N.1.1 | Who can see a student's record
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** All AIDTM students · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Helpdesk policy written for this project (not issued by AIDTM)
**Questions:** Can I see another student's fees or grades? | Can my parents get my information? | Can a classmate or CR see my record? | Who at the Institute can see my data?
**Keywords:** privacy, view other student record, classmate fees, parent access, confidential, who can see my data

**Your private record:** fees and dues, scholarship, grades and CGPA, attendance, disciplinary matters, support requests, contact details and bank details.

| Who | What they can see |
|---|---|
| You (signed in) | Your entire record |
| Another student, the Class Representative or a friend | Nothing, even if they know your name or enrollment number |
| Parent or guardian | Information only through the contact registered with the Institute, and only with your written consent |
| Faculty | Grades and attendance for their own courses |
| Program Office | Academic records, requests and contact details |
| Accounts | Fee and scholarship records |
| Placement Office | Profile, CGPA and placement records for placement purposes |
| Employers, banks and universities | Verification only with your written consent |

The helpdesk refuses any request for another student's private data, however it is worded, and records the attempt (N.1.5).

**Public information** is available to everyone: policies, calendars, programme details and committee members.


### N.1.2 | Sensitive changes need verification and approval
**Type:** table · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Helpdesk policy written for this project (not issued by AIDTM); placement opt-out rule from SIP Policy §8 (official)
**Questions:** How do I change my phone number or email? | How do I change my bank account for refunds? | Why does my change need approval? | What happens if my change request is rejected? | How long does approval take?
**Keywords:** change my details, update phone number, change email, change bank account, name correction, approval required, pending approval

| Change | Verification | Approved by | Usual time |
|---|---|---|---|
| Registered mobile, email or address | Signed-in identity, plus confirmation of the exact new value | Program Office | 2 working days |
| Refund bank account (bank name and last 4 digits only in chat) | Cancelled cheque or bank letter submitted to Accounts | Accounts / Program Office | 3 working days |
| Name or date-of-birth correction | Original certificate (10th/12th or passport) | Program Office | 5 working days |
| Programme switch | Written application (D.4.1) | Academic Committee | Next committee meeting |
| Withdrawing a scholarship application | Signed-in identity | Program Office | 2 working days |
| Opting out of placements | Email to the Program Office and the Placement Office (official) | Program Office | Final once approved |

**How it works:**

1. You ask for the change and confirm the exact new value.
2. A request is filed with status **Pending Approval**. Nothing changes yet.
3. An authorised officer approves or rejects it.
4. Only an approved change is applied. A rejected one is never applied, and your record stays as it was.

You are told the outcome either way. Every step is written to the audit log (N.1.5).


### N.1.3 | OTPs, passwords and other secrets
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** All AIDTM students · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Sample security policy for this project; credential rules from Handbook Annexure X
**Questions:** Will the helpdesk ask for my OTP or password? | I shared my password by mistake, what now? | How do I reset my portal password?
**Keywords:** OTP, password, PIN, share password, forgot password, login problem, account hacked, phishing

AIDTM staff and the student helpdesk will never ask for a password, OTP, PIN, CVV or full card number. Never type these into chat, email or forms. If shared by mistake, they are not stored or used, and the student should change the password immediately. Lab and internet credentials are issued by the Program Office and the IT Officer. For a password reset, contact the IT Officer (Mr. Jignesh Parekh) in person with your I-Card, or raise an "IT & Login" request. Report any message pretending to be AIDTM that asks for credentials.


### N.1.4 | What the helpdesk remembers about a student
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Sample data-handling policy for this project
**Questions:** What does the helpdesk remember about me? | Can I make the helpdesk forget my data? | Does the helpdesk store my OTP?
**Keywords:** what do you remember, data stored, memory, forget me, delete my data

To give continuity, the helpdesk may remember stable, useful facts a student shares, such as their programme and batch or a preferred language or contact method. It does not keep passwords, OTPs or other credentials, payment card details, health information, or temporary remarks. A student can ask the helpdesk to forget stored preferences. Official records, such as fees and grades, are held by the Institute's systems rather than the helpdesk's memory.


### N.1.5 | Audit trail of record access and changes
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** All AIDTM students · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Sample data-privacy policy written for this project
**Questions:** Is there a log of who accessed my data? | Can I see the audit log of my record?
**Keywords:** audit log, who accessed my data, record of changes, transparency

Every access to a private record and every attempted change is logged. The log records who made the request, the time, what was attempted, whether it was allowed or blocked, the approval decision for sensitive changes, and the final result. Audit records are kept by the Institute for review in disputes and compliance checks. A student may ask the Program Office for the log of changes to their own record.

# Part O — Admissions and joining

Eligibility and selection, documents at registration, your enrollment number, ID card and email, and the bridge course.


## O.1 Admissions and joining


### O.1.1 | Admission eligibility, selection and seat confirmation
**Type:** procedure · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Helpdesk policy written for this project (not issued by AIDTM); registration fee, intake and contacts official (Admission Offer Annexure II, AIDTM website)
**Questions:** What is the eligibility for PGDM at AIDTM? | Which entrance exams are accepted? | How are students selected? | How do I confirm my seat? | Who do I contact for admissions?
**Keywords:** admission eligibility, entrance exam, CAT, XAT, MAT, CMAT, GMAT, selection process, interview, seat confirmation

**Eligibility:**

- A bachelor's degree in any discipline with at least 50% aggregate (45% for reserved categories, as per AICTE).
- A valid score in CAT, XAT, MAT, CMAT, ATMA or GMAT.
- Final-year students may apply provisionally and must submit their degree results by the date in the offer letter.

**Selection:**

| Component | Weight |
|---|---|
| Entrance test percentile | 40% |
| Academic record (10th, 12th, graduation) | 20% |
| Written ability test | 10% |
| Personal interview | 20% |
| Work experience and diversity of background | 10% |

The written test and interview are held on campus or online.

**Offer and confirmation:**

- Selected candidates receive an admission offer with the fee structure (Annexure II, F.1.2).
- The seat is confirmed by paying the registration fee of INR 2,00,000 (official).
- The 1st installment, with the INR 50,000 caution deposit, is due by 15 July (official).

**Intake:** BDA 90 seats, AI & DS 60 seats (official).

**Contact:** admissions@aidtm.ac.in, +91 63589 75222, Admission Manager Mr. Haard Trivedi, Senior Admission Counsellors Ms. Raksha Desai and Mr. Aditya Thakare.


### O.1.2 | Documents required at admission and registration
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Sample list built for this project; the anti-ragging undertaking is official (Handbook Annexure VIII)
**Questions:** What documents are needed at admission? | Is the anti-ragging undertaking compulsory? | Do I need a medical certificate?
**Keywords:** documents for admission, registration documents, undertaking, affidavit, migration

At registration, students submit: 10th and 12th mark sheets, graduation mark sheets and degree or provisional certificate, entrance test scorecard, photo ID (Aadhaar or passport), passport-size photographs, caste or category certificate if applicable, a medical fitness certificate, a migration or transfer certificate where issued, the signed SIP and Placement Policy declaration, and the anti-ragging undertaking signed by the student and parent (compulsory before attending classes). Originals are verified and returned.


### O.1.3 | Enrollment number, ID card and student email
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Sample procedure built for this project; ID card rules are official (Handbook)
**Questions:** What is my enrollment number? | What if I lose my ID card? | What is my student email used for?
**Keywords:** enrollment number, student ID card, lost ID card, duplicate ID, student email, login

Each student receives an enrollment number, an Institute ID card and an Institute email account at registration. The ID card must be carried at all times on campus and in exams. A lost or damaged ID card is replaced within 3 working days on payment of INR 500 through an "IT & Login" or "Certificates & Documents" request. Official communication goes to the Institute email, which students must check daily. The same credentials are used for the student portal, the LMS and campus Wi-Fi.


### O.1.4 | Bridge course before Trimester I
**Type:** rule · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** AUGMENTED · **Source:** Dates from Student Handbook Annexure II (official); content and rules are a helpdesk description
**Questions:** What is the bridge course? | When is the bridge course? | Is the bridge course compulsory? | What is taught in the bridge course?
**Keywords:** bridge course, induction, pre-programme, orientation, refresher

**Dates (official):** 17 July 2025 (Thursday) to 31 July 2025 (Thursday), just before Trimester I began on 1 August 2025.

**Purpose:** to bring students from different academic backgrounds (B.Com, BBA, BE, B.Tech, B.Sc., BCA, B.A., B.Voc) to a common starting level.

**Content:**

- Python and programming basics.
- Excel for analysis.
- Statistics and mathematics refresher (algebra, probability, basic calculus for ML).
- Business fundamentals.
- Communication.

**Attendance and assessment:** attendance is compulsory and recorded as part of induction. There are no credits or grades, but a diagnostic test at the end helps faculty plan extra support sessions in Trimester I.


---

# Part X — Appendices

Quick-reference numbers, a glossary of abbreviations, and (in the PDF) an index of every question this handbook answers.


## X.1 Reference tables


### X.1.1 | Key numbers quick-reference
**Type:** reference · **Applies to:** PGDM-BDA, PGDM-AIDS · **Status:** OFFICIAL-DERIVED · **Source:** Derived from all official documents
**Questions:** Give me the key numbers | What are the important limits and numbers to remember? | Quick facts about rules
**Keywords:** important numbers, quick facts, cheat sheet, minimum, maximum, limits

Attendance: 100% expected; at most 30% absence with permission; 70% minimum to sit end-terms. Grades: A 3.55-4.33, B 2.55-3.54, C 1.55-2.54, D 0.55-1.54, F below 0.55; maximum CGPA 4.33. To graduate: CGPA of at least 2.17, no F or I, no dues, within at most 4 years. Percentage classes: 3.04 or above = 70%+, 2.60 or above = 60%+, 2.17 or above = 50%+. Credits: 1 credit = 10 contact hours; a 3-credit course is about 100 hours of effort. Grade dispute: within 4 days with the faculty, then 2 days to the Dean (Handbook §10.3.1). Annexure V appeal: 1 week to the faculty, 1 week for the reply, 1 week to the Programme Chair, Academic Committee within 15 days, decision communicated in 2 days. Grievance: 15 days to Committee, 7 days to communicate, 1 week to appeal to the Dean, 15 days for the Dean's decision. Fees: INR 10,00,000 tuition, 5 payments of INR 2,00,000, INR 50,000 caution deposit. Late fee: INR 100/200/400 per day for up to 2, 2-4 and 4-6 weeks; 6 weeks maximum; then INR 2,000 per day, capped at 100% of fees. Library: 3 books, 14 days, 1 renewal, INR 5 per day up to 60 days; replace a lost book within 15 days; treated as lost after 90 days. Fines: mobile INR 500 spot, INR 2,000 / 5,000 for return; I-Card INR 100; hall ticket INR 100; ragging INR 25,000. Exams: 15 minutes before (AI & DS) or 10 minutes (Handbook); no leaving before 30 minutes or in the last 5 (AI & DS) or 15 (Handbook) minutes. Placements: at most 5 interviews; 1.5x CTC rule; SIP about 5 months, capstone about 4 months. Intake: BDA 90, AI & DS 60. Placement stats: 100% placed, 20 LPA highest, 8 LPA lowest, 90% PPOs.


### X.1.2 | Glossary of abbreviations and terms
**Type:** reference · **Applies to:** All · **Status:** OFFICIAL-DERIVED · **Source:** Terms used across AIDTM documents and this handbook
**Questions:** What does SIP mean? | What is PPO? | What is CTC or LPA? | What does UFM stand for? | What is SGRC or WGRC? | What is an I grade? | What is AICTE?
**Keywords:** glossary, abbreviations, meaning of, full form, acronyms, SIP, PPO, CTC, LPA, UFM, CGPA

| Term | Meaning |
|---|---|
| AICTE | All India Council for Technical Education; approves AIDTM's PGDM programmes |
| AIDTM | Adani Institute of Digital Technology Management |
| AIHER | Adani Institute of Higher Education and Research, which awards the Diploma in Strategic Management |
| AI & DS / AIDS | PGDM in Artificial Intelligence & Data Science |
| BDA | PGDM in Big Data Analytics |
| CGPA / GPA | Cumulative / trimester grade point average (maximum 4.33) |
| CP | Class participation |
| CR | Class Representative |
| CTC / LPA | Cost to company (annual salary package) / lakhs per annum |
| EPAAA | Executive Program in AI, Analytics & Automation |
| I grade | Incomplete; a temporary grade that becomes F if not cleared |
| I-Card | Institute identity card |
| IJP | Internal job posting (Adani Group) |
| KOHA | The library management system |
| NOC | No-objection certificate |
| PGDM | Post Graduate Diploma in Management |
| POSH | Prevention of sexual harassment |
| PPO | Pre-placement offer, made by the SIP company |
| SGRC | Students Grievance Redressal Committee |
| SIP | Summer Internship Program |
| SR | Support request (ticket), e.g. SR-2026-0101 |
| UFM | Unfair means, i.e. cheating in an examination |
| UTR | Unique transaction reference of a bank transfer |
| VF | Visiting faculty |
| WGRC | Women Grievance Redressal Committee |
| Trimester / term | One of six academic periods; students often say semester |
